## Mathematical conventions and corrections

The paper keeps the full indexed peripheral source maps, their conjugacy ambiguities and the orientation line. It distinguishes a prescribed section homomorphism from its image subgroup by explicitly writing the quotient labels. Discrete, full profinite and pro-p maps have separate domains, and every use of a completion map includes its injectivity or exactness argument. The first page gives the global notation concordance. Each numbered assertion is followed by section/folio/scan metadata; numerical evidence for a transcription alternative appears in the separate readings register and is never called a settled reading.

Several corrections drive the later proofs. The sphere with three punctures has free group of rank two, so the abelian bound on scan116 is nu<=2. The torus requires the same extension-class correction as the annulus. A reflection section can extend to different maximal finite stabilizers; the explicit type(0,3) NEC amalgam proves this in the discrete group and its completion. A finite subgroup of the mapping class group does not determine an exceptional-type affine extension: the retained extension has to be part of the index. Arbitrary functors between peripheral categories also include maps induced by no surface homeomorphism; C.1 gives the exact core restriction used in the proved equivalence. Finite-point drilling removes isolated rotation stabilizers but leaves mirror arcs; C.14 records the resulting obstruction rather than treating a reflection as a rotation.

The peripheral category uses E/L_i with the specified right action of E_i/L_i. The proposed E/E_i replacement loses the map to the operator group; C.2 gives an actual double-cover example and a finite certificate. The abstract-presentation question retains the phrase that the displayed relation generates all relations. A.11 proves the standard completion under that axiom and separately refutes the weaker condition that a generating tuple merely satisfy the relation. These are connected by explicit quotient maps in the proof.

Actual stabilizing operators approximate q_n after composition with h_n, uniformly on all contraction and inverse images of that compact set; no common distortion bound for h_n is assumed. Small L-transports and exact commutation give compact-open convergence to a global quotient, whose full hybrid equation forces the unique limit. A diagonal argument gives closure continuity and inverse continuity. This infinite-component contraction agrees exactly with X_dagger. The independent proof audit passed. Arbitrarily many H-inequivalent remaining components, noncocompact quotients and extension through the exterior nonopen regions remain explicit.

F.9 supplies an exact nontrivial common kernel word w=m_minus_one m_i m_one_inverse m_i_inverse for the twelve seven-point maps. The actual A4 coordinates are normalized into Conf6(C), with a specified based quotient to M0,7. The three braid words satisfy AB=BC through the faithful Artin free-group substitution. ## Remaining admissible infinite-action zero-level question

C.11 gives explicit infinite cyclic actions on every hyperbolic surface containing a disc, with disconnected actual centralizer and with nonconjugate actions inducing the same outer action. C.23 supplies a precise infinite non-realization theorem and checks its hypotheses. C.25 gives a faithful infinite cyclic sphere action whose centralizer comparison has kernel2Z on fundamental groups; C.24 independently computes the full untruncated finite C3 sphere comparison using the arithmetic fracture square and the primary mapping-space theorem.

The source in C.1 follows scan264 and excludes an unpunctured genus-zero component. Thus C.25 is retained with its enlarged sphere target and does not complete the admissible infinite-group request. The exact map for an admitted surface is pi1(C_A(H),1) to pi1(A,1)^Gamma. Its target is zero in the hyperbolic types, or the invariant subgroup of Z,Z,Z² in the plane, annulus and torus. The sphere example's common axis-rotation loop maps as Z to Z to Z to C2 when its two poles are successively unmarked. The first two maps are identities and the last is reduction modulo2; adding one or both punctures therefore kills the exhibited kernel. Adding the sphere to the peripheral-diagram recipe gives the further exact map C2 to0, which is also recorded without identifying these targets. The literal admissible level0 existential question remains unsettled; its hyperbolic subcase is pi1(C_Homeo0(U)(H))=0 for arbitrary operator groups.

The supported-rotation construction only detects a component permutation. Adding finitely many fixed points leads to aspherical configuration spaces and does not create the needed pi2. The canonical lift of Homeo0(U) to Homeo(U-tilde), commuting with the deck group, is constructed and is a continuous injection; its intersection with the full lifted-action centralizer is exactly the original centralizer. A geodesic interpolation has neither established equivariance for arbitrary homeomorphisms nor an established homeomorphism property. Those two missing properties are explicitly recorded. Thus neither a universal contraction nor a hyperbolic counterexample is asserted.

C.26 proves that the centralizer of a transitive subgroup on a closed genus-at-least-two surface is finite. The open mapping theorem for Polish actions identifies the orbit with the stabilizer coset space; evaluation identifies the centralizer with a closed subset of that compact surface. Its action is free, while every element isotopic to the identity has nonzero Lefschetz number and a fixed point. Projection to the discrete mapping-class group is therefore an injection of a compact group, giving finiteness. Consequently a centralizer loop whose own values generate a transitive subgroup reduces to the already proved finite-action case. The exact primary input is Gomez–Pinedo–Uzcategui, Theorem3.5, whose statement and proof were opened and checked with all ordinary-action hypotheses.

The same numbered result gives a complete counterexample to a weaker proposed shortcut: the closed torsion-free Polish group of integer-valued L1 functions modulo constant integers has fundamental group Z. Support compression contracts its universal cover, and the indicator-function loop generates its fundamental group. Integer medians give its closed isometric embedding into L1 modulo real constants; dual-ball evaluation, Cantor pullback, interpolation and an explicit tanh shear then embed it closedly into the disc homeomorphism group. Its loop contracts in the larger real shear group. This proves that closedness and torsion-freeness alone cannot give the required fundamental-group injection. The corollary to C.39 now excludes its realization as an exact full centralizer: every centralizer containing this abelian subgroup contracts its generating loop, so inclusion induces the zero map on its fundamental group Z. Equality with that centralizer would induce an isomorphism. This closes that particular proposed counterexample route while retaining the general C.12 obligation.

C.27 gives a conjugacy-natural median contraction for every loop in a line centralizer, allowing orientation reversal by the operators. C.28 proves the orbit classification for a group generated by a compact plane loop: open orbits, points, Jordan curves, and intrinsic lines. The proof uses compact Peano exhaustions, the winding-number argument for a moving Jordan curve, and the countability of dendrite branch points together with transitivity; it does not assume a foliation. The line parametrization embeds every compact interval and makes the restricted loop compact-open continuous, while retaining possible global accumulation of the orbit.

C.29 proves the injective winding map for every circle centralizer and its natural zero-winding contraction. Evaluation at two points of a Jordan curve gives an exact difference-angle winding equal to the circle winding; the canonical hyperbolic plane loop is ambient-null, so every circle orbit has zero winding. C.30 proves that the centralizer of a transitive Polish action on a surface is locally compact and acts freely and properly. Its application to an open orbit uses the closure of the actual restricted operator image, retaining the kernel and its topology. C.31 proves uniform track control at common fixed points and the exact inverse-loop identity for the median. C.32 proves the discrete proper open-orbit case by a quotient-surface contraction and its specified covering lift. C.33 proves every proper closed-operator case, using the faithful product action for the Hilbert–Smith theorem, the free-action slice theorem and explicit coordinates in each Lie-group dimension. In dimension one it retains every component and the possible inversion, removes the translation cocycle by an explicit function, and contracts the resulting function and base-homeomorphism factors. Thus every individual orbit is handled intrinsically.

C.34 proves continuity on the entire closed nonopen locus. A folded nearby arc produces a cap whose two first-exit endpoints lie outside a small rectangle. A short original-loop segment moves the nonfixed limit point across the cap extremum while its whole track remains in the rectangle, a contradiction to intrinsic path continuity. This no-excess argument gives convergence of intervals, prefix endpoints and medians. Circle limits use only a lift of the compact point-track to a collar and local lifts inside a single covering rectangle. The inverse-loop identity proves joint continuity of the inverse family. Hyperbolic loops with no open lifted orbit therefore contract in the exact original centralizer by deck descent. C.35 supplies an exact obstruction to extending this fixed median prescription. The positive-diagonal action on a simplex has interior translation coordinates(a,b) and edge characters a,b,a-b. Doubling the simplex and removing one fixed vertex gives a plane action. The parameter loop0→(1,1)→(1,0)→0 is globally equivariantly contractible by scaling. At one median parameter its edge translations are(1,0,0), outside the exact restriction image(A,B,A-B); the annihilator residual is1. Every equivariant interior map, including one exchanging the two sheets, has that restriction image. Thus a successful global proof must allow changing the nonopen-locus contraction. C.36 advances the frontier comparison: the closure of the loop-generated group maps by an actual open Polish quotient onto the two-dimensional simply transitive Lie centralizer. Its kernel fixes the entire orbit closure, so the quotient acts continuously there. The based universal-cover exponential contraction descends to that closure. Every shared boundary orbit has exactly the same effective Lie image from both sides, and the quotient homomorphisms intertwine the unique based lifts and exponentials. Thus these contractions agree on overlaps and glue with continuous inverse on every finite union. C.37 supplies the uniform continuity at infinitely accumulating closures described next. Compatible extension to the exterior of their closed union, including the dimension-zero/one operator images and the remaining nonopen strata, remains C.12.

C.37 proves the contraction on X2, the entire closure of the union of dimension-two proper operator open orbits. The exact input is a three-word equation: every fractional endpoint in R² or the simply connected positive affine group is a path difference, or its product with the endpoint. The signed-measure theorem of Grandoni–Ravi–Singh–Zenklusen, author-version Theorem4.7 and Section4.3.2, gives the planar equation for polygonal paths; uniform approximation gives every continuous path. Explicit affine coordinates turn left multiplication by the endpoint flow into horizontal translation. The equality is retained in the universal covering group. Actual plane word isotopies then lie in one compact family. On every nonopen limit orbit the metabelian identity passes from the open orbits, and the path-connected transitive image is proved to be R, S1 or the positive affine group. For each integer n, the residual of the nth power after removing floor(nr) endpoint powers remains a three-word element. Bounded logarithms force the exact fractional limit on lines. On circles, a residual word loop is null in every approximating Lie group; its two-point configuration winding passes to the limit, so the residual equation holds over R and retains the full winding integer. Density proves joint continuity throughout X2, and the inverse loop proves inverse continuity. This resolves the infinite-accumulation issue for that closed set. Its contraction still needs compatible extension to the exterior of X2; the median prescription there cannot be assumed compatible, by C.35.

C.38 proves that the full compact-open centralizer of a transitive nonabelian action on a line or circle is discrete cyclic: trivial or infinite cyclic on a line, finite cyclic on a circle. The evaluation map is an explicit homeomorphism onto a closed normalizer quotient. The proper free action yields the actual regular covering. In the plane problem the exact restriction sequence retains the operator stabilizer, its kernel and its discrete image; no continuity from an inherited plane topology to a recurrent intrinsic line topology is assumed. This supplies the stated one-dimensional quotients, while the transverse relative-extension problem in C.12 remains.

C.39 supplies a complete global contraction for ambient-null plane loops with pairwise commuting values. On each intrinsic orbit the closure is a free transitive abelian Lie group, and the actual loop-generated image equals that closure. The based universal-cover lift has zero endpoint. One compact family of actual three-word plane isotopies represents every scaled endpoint on every orbit type. At a mixed-type limit, nth powers minus floor(nr) endpoint powers have uniformly bounded word representatives. Line translations give the exact real limit; on circles the residual word loop has zero two-point winding, so the real lifted equation holds with its winding integer retained. Every word limit therefore has the required fractional action. Applying the same argument to the inverse loop gives joint inverse continuity. The disk filling lifts through the explicit map D2 times the universal covering surface; each commutator with an operator lift or deck transformation is a continuous deck-valued function equal to the identity at the base point. Descent supplies the contraction on every connected admissible surface. On hyperbolic surfaces every commuting-valued centralizer loop contracts. This proof does not replace an arbitrary loop by a commuting-valued representative; that reduction, or a general compatible extension, remains the exact C.12 issue.

C.40 proves the further case of a loop whose generated closure is locally compact in the actual induced homeomorphism-group topology. That closure is connected and lies in the closed centralizer. Its faithful action on the surface times R makes it a Lie group by the checked Hilbert–Smith theorem. Borel’s exact multiplication coordinates K times R^d give an identity-fixing deformation into a connected compact subgroup. Haar averaging gives a bi-invariant metric; its universal cover is geodesically complete by the explicit connection formula. A geodesic to the lifted endpoint of the loop projects to a one-parameter loop in the same based homotopy class. The entire homotopy stays in the centralizer, and C.39 contracts that representative when the original ambient class vanishes. Thus each locally compact centralizer has injective fundamental-group comparison, and every such closure case on a hyperbolic surface has trivial loop class. Root opened Borel’s original scan and visually checked the product formula on printed page271; sections3–4, pages277–278, give the stated reductions. The general closure need not satisfy the local compactness hypothesis established for this result; no change of topology is made.

C.41 proves the fundamental-group injection for arbitrary operator subgroups with a dense orbit on every connected admissible surface. In the plane, the union of open loop-generated orbits is operator-invariant. A dense operator orbit meets every such open orbit O, and its intersection with O is exactly one orbit of the O-stabilizing operators. Their proper closed image has a closed orbit containing that dense subset, so it is transitive and two-dimensional. The whole open-orbit union is consequently the dimension-two union of C.37, and its closure is the plane. With no open orbits, C.34 already gives the contraction. For a general surface, the exact ambient disk family lifts, and the full group of operator lifts has a dense orbit equal to the inverse image of the original one. The plane contraction descends. This proof requires no local compactness assumption on the original operator group or on the global loop-generated closure. C.12 remains for general actions without a dense orbit and loops for which the other proved reductions have not been obtained.

## Source verification and manuscript readings

Every bibliography entry is individually marked verified or unverified. The notes identify the exact opened source and theorem numbers. The prior LM2,LM3,LM3b and companion returns were read as claims and checked where used, never as proof by status label. In particular, the normal descent peripheral step, the closed-loop centralizers and the category/strict-action comparisons are supplied in full here.

The prompt's description of Boggi's paper as unrefereed is superseded by the publisher record opened in this session: publication23September2026, Sao Paulo Journal of Mathematical Sciences20,Article35. TheoremC's solvable conjugacy restriction is retained. F.7 records the exact unverified closure-intersection step and supplies an independent solvable proof; publication status does not settle that step. The finite-shadow papers and the Collas–Philip–Yamaguchi and Yamaguchi2026 manuscripts retain their actual preprint status unless a separately opened publication record supports a change.

The readings CSV records all mathematical evidence bearing on the doubtful manuscript words. It includes the orientation hypothesis, the abelian bracket, the direction of devissage, the cancellation marks around the withdrawn assertions, the fibre/peripheral distinctions, and the tube presentation. Mathematical consistency is only evidence for a reading. The exact alternative text remains recorded, and manuscript scans were inspected for the relevant entries.

## Assembly, reproducibility and layout verification

The assembler checks that every numbered statement has exactly one results row, that no claim label is duplicated, and that every citation key is present. It hashes every active source fragment, metadata file, bibliography input and readings file; packaging rejects any change after assembly. It takes statement text and status from the actual paper fragments, so stale shorter metadata cannot change the registered mathematical assertion. It normalizes proof locations to the self-contained paper and includes the stated computation locations.

All accepted computations use exact integers, rational numbers, finite words, finite permutations or finite fields. A finite search is used only for the exact finite assertion it exhausts. Infinite conclusions have separate proofs. The code and captured outputs are included together; primary numerical input has source attribution and hashes. The final packaging manifest records actual files and hashes and excludes interpreter caches.

## The resulting finite-step table

Write P_m=P/P^[m], m>=2, where the derived subgroups are closed. All groups in this table are full profinite or pro-Sigma groups, with nonempty Sigma; the full profinite and single-prime cases are included. A standard handle generator means a generator in a geometric surface basis.

| Hyperbolic type | Centre and torsion | Open subgroup centralizers | Peripheral C(L^n), N(L), and transporters in an action extension | Standard handle C(<x>^n) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| nu>=2 | Centre-free and torsion-free | Trivial | L at every depth; extension normalizer/transporter is E_s | <x> at every depth |
| nu=1,m=2 | Centre-free and torsion-free | Trivial | P_2′, strictly containing L; extension normalizer/transporter is P_2′E_s | <x> |
| nu=1,m>=3 | Centre-free and torsion-free | Trivial | L; extension normalizer/transporter is E_s | <x> |
| nu=0,g>=2 | Centre-free and torsion-free | Trivial | No peripheral indices | <x> at every depth |

Distinct peripheral indices remain noncommensurable in all hyperbolic punctured finite-step quotients. The exceptional groups1,1,Zhat,Zhat² are already abelian, so their finite-step quotients are unchanged and A.4 applies verbatim. In an annulus action, N_E(L)=Trans_E(L^d,L)=E; equality with the peripheral E_s is equivalent to fixing both ends. On the plane the peripheral mixed group has a noninjective map into E, with kernel Z in the discrete case and Zhat after completion; using its image gives the equality, but it cannot be identified with a subgroup before that kernel is removed.

## Proof details requiring particular care

### Closed solvable groups: A.19

The initial partial bound C_{P_m}(H) subset P_m^[m-1] is retained in A.18, but the obstruction is resolved by A.19. The proof starts with an explicit continuous Magnus homomorphism F→R^r⋊F_ab. Its kernel is proved to be F″ by factoring every finite metabelian quotient through a finite wreath embedding. Thus no exactness of an unspecified completed cellular chain complex is assumed.

For the surface relator, its image is the Fox vector v=(1-b1,a1-1,…,1-bg,ag-1). The closed normal relator module is exactly Rv, because Rv is a compact image. This proves the injection P′/P″→R^(2g)/Rv. Multiplication by a1^n-1 is injective on this quotient: the independent a2-1 coordinate is a nonzerodivisor modulo a1^n-1. Both nonzerodivisor assertions are proved by finite-coefficient cyclic orbit sums; choosing M divisible by the Sigma-part of n and passing from C_M to C_{kM} makes each orbit fibre sum k equal coefficients modulo k. This explicitly includes p=2,3 and integers n divisible by p.

Finite class-two groups with commutator module (wedge²A)/(sum a_i wedge b_i) force the abelianization of a centralizer element onto the selected generator's line. This proves the metabelian base case. For higher depth, every finite abelian cover has a lift x^d of the selected nonseparating simple curve. Its integral homology pushes forward to d[x], so it is again nonseparating and can be made a standard handle generator. A.15 justifies both required intersections of closed derived subgroups and products. Induction proves all depths, and two independent standard-generator powers then prove slimness.

This proof does not establish the broader arbitrary-characteristic assertion discussed as unknown in the supplied Yamaguchi preprint. It establishes exactly the characteristic-zero topological surface groups requested here. No priority claimed.

### One-puncture peripheral threshold: A.16

Finite abelian covers of a once-punctured surface have at least three cusps after choosing the cofinal family of deck degrees>=3. In the cusp homology module Z_Sigma^Q/Z_Sigma(sum e_q), an equality n e_q=a e_1 forces q=1 and a=n. This puts every peripheral power-centralizer into the derived subgroup. At depth2 that subgroup is abelian and is the entire centralizer. At depth>=3 the same cover makes c a primitive free-basis element, so the finite-rank free theorem at one smaller depth applies. A.15 then descends the result back to the original quotient. The strict metabelian inclusion is certified by explicit UT4 matrices at p=2,3,5 and by the same symbolic matrix calculation at any p in Sigma.

### Exact source applications

- Mochizuki–Tamagawa, Definition1.2, Remark1.2.2 and Proposition1.4 apply to hyperbolic geometric surface groups over an algebraically closed characteristic-zero field and an arbitrary nontrivial full formation. The ordinary/full/pro-p presentation comparisons and the deduction of torsion-freeness from finite p-cohomological dimension are written out.
- Mochizuki2004 Lemma1.3.7 gives commensurable terminality for both full and pro-l cusp inertia. A centralizer of an integer power lies in that commensurator, giving A.7.
- Mochizuki2007 Proposition1.2(i) identifies underlying edges when an intersection is open in one. It does not literally identify arbitrary chosen conjugate subgroup representatives. A.5 therefore proves its one-sided transporter assertion using exact power-centralizers and compact recurrence, with no strengthened quotation of Proposition1.2(i).
- Closed base-loop centralizers in the unquotiented completion are obtained twice, from Boggi–Zalesskii2019 Proposition3.6 and from Mochizuki2007 Proposition1.2(ii) after a one-node degeneration. Genus2 is checked explicitly: its normalization has genus1 and two inverse branches, hence remains stable and hyperbolic.
- The finite-rank part of Yamaguchi2026 Theorem1.5 is used in A.14. Its finite semidirect-product step, metabelian module step and descent to finite abelian covers were read. No infinite-rank limiting argument is imported. A.17 independently checks the cover/transfer proof of the centre and torsion statements. A.19 independently proves the precise Magnus embedding used in the closed argument.
- Yagasaki's actual supplied TeX was read. The compact marked-surface case needed for A.12 lies in Lemma2.1; the finite marked set is a compact subpolyhedron. We also read Theorem1.1 and the local-extension theorem. This avoids treating a statement about diffeomorphisms as one about compact-open homeomorphisms without a comparison.
- Farb–Margalit Theorem4.6 includes punctures/boundary and has an explicit extended-group remark. Theorem8.8 is the extended punctured Dehn–Nielsen–Baer theorem. A.12 uses the inverse-track convention needed to make point-pushing a homomorphism and checks its induced inner automorphism. The book's p235 informal phrase about a unique representative fixing the distinguished loop is not used: conjugation by that loop supplies many representatives. The quotient automorphism is unique because those residual conjugations die upon filling.

## Corrections and typed comparisons

The bracket nu<=3 on scan116 is false at the pair of pants; the correct bound is nu<=2. The rank2g+nu-1 requires nu>=1. The admitted plane retains its noninjective peripheral source map, while the closed sphere is excluded from the nontrivial orientation-by-group-cohomology definition. The early cohomological orientation marking is related to the later covariant orientation line by an explicit dual map, rather than an unmarked inversion of its character.

The torus needs the same H² extension-class correction as the annulus. The pure orientation-preserving index on scan189 is exactly2nu!, where nu counts the old punctures. In the abelian filling exception, forgetting annulus peripheral indices and orientation loses a C2; retaining the complete data restores the four-element target and the isomorphism. The scan194 abelian labels refer to filled types; both drilled groups there are F2.

The phrase relation génératrice on scan318 gives the full profinite presentation, hence the explicit isomorphism to the standard completion. The Zhat² example refutes only the weakened requirement that a generating tuple satisfy the equation. It is not presented as a counterexample to the actual full-presentation axiom.

A.20 has all four domains and the completion comparison maps written out. The discrete filling isomorphism gives an isomorphism of the full completions of the two discrete automorphism groups. It also gives the commutative square with the separately defined normalized filling map on all continuous automorphisms. Neither vertical surjectivity nor a bottom-arrow isomorphism is needed for A(d). For odd p the proposed replacement of the upper full completions by pro-p completions has an explicit obstruction: an orientation-reversing involution is killed in a pro-p group while acting by -1 on the pro-p orientation line.

## Sources actually read and searches

The primary local PDFs for Mochizuki2004, Mochizuki2007, Mochizuki–Tamagawa2008 and Nakamura1994 were extracted and their relevant theorem statements and proofs read. Titles were checked on first pages: the 2008 article is *The Algebraic and Anabelian Geometry of Configuration Spaces*; the 1994 article is *Galois Rigidity of Pure Sphere Braid Groups and Profinite Calculus*. Publisher metadata for the former and for Mochizuki2007 was opened. Boggi–Zalesskii2019 was opened in its author arXiv PDF and Proposition3.6 read. Yamaguchi's supplied source and arXivv4 HTML were opened. Yagasaki's primary supplied source was read.

The Farb–Margalit author URL returned403 and the Pisa/KIAS mirrors timed out. An actual primary book copy was then opened successfully at https://euclid.nmu.edu/~joshthom/teaching/ma589/farbmarg.pdf; the case-sensitive lowercase path matters. The precise statements and adjacent remarks were inspected there. All bibliography entries in this Part A file are consequently marked verified. An original Ihara1999 proof was searched for while auditing the Magnus step; its bibliography and RIMS1177 listing were found, but no original text was obtained. No claim is made that it was read, it is not a relied-on bibliography entry, and the required embedding was proved directly in A.19.

Before recording the finite-step arguments, searches included combinations of “finite-step solvable surface group centralizer,” “profinite metabelian surface group centralizer,” “peripheral product commutators solvable quotient,” the exact Yamaguchi title/arXiv identifier, and “Ihara Blanchfield Lyndon Theorem A-2.” The actual proof stands independently of any claim that those searches are exhaustive. No newness or priority claim is made.

The prior LM3 return and review were consulted as leads and compared with the new proofs of Lemme5 and the two closed-loop source reductions. Their status labels are not used as authority. The initially suspected absence of prior-return source files was rechecked and disproved; the files are present and no missing-file limitation remains.

## Corrections carrying consequences for other parts

- The unique maximal-section assertion requires a nontrivial positive part. Reflections already violate it in the smallest hyperbolic type (0,3). The same concrete discrete subgroups survive in the completion.
- The valid positive discrete normalizer equality N=C does not make the torsion in C into a group. The explicit punctured-torus example separates these claims by their actual inclusion and product maps.
- The scan-179 uniqueness expectation fails for a free glide involution as well as for C_n×C2 of arbitrary order.
- An outer subgroup in an abelian type does not determine its lift extension. The same order-two subgroup in GL2Z has a two-section realization and a zero-section realization.
- Formula (52) presents a group with a free F_g factor. The quotient F_g→Z^g, with its explicit equivariant abelianization map, is the correction needed to obtain the stated geometric semidirect product.

## Detailed Part C and special-group record

## C(a): categories, fidelity, and homotopy fixed points

C.1 defines the corrected source and target 2-groupoids, their arrows, 2-arrows, peripheral fibre condition, and comparison. The literal target in scans 265–268 contains arbitrary diagram functors. It has a trivial endofunctor of the three-punctured-sphere peripheral diagram, inducing zero on H1; no homeomorphism can induce it. Thus equivalence with that unrestricted full target is refuted. Replacing its arrows by equivalences is an explicit core construction, with precise maps retained.

C.2 constructs the coset exit category and recovers peripheral subgroups as arrow stabilizers. Its equivariant Hom-set is E/L_i, with right action by E_i/L_i. E/E_i does not have the required projection to Gamma when the puncture stabilizer is nontrivial. The covering z -> z^2 of P1 minus {0,infinity,1,-1} gives an actual geometric example, with L=<a^2> and E_i=<a>. The finite C4 certificate checks the same projection obstruction.

C.3 proves the four fidelity tests directly from functors of transporter fundamental groupoids. Levels 0, 1, 2 test, respectively, pi1 injectivity; pi0 injectivity plus pi1 isomorphism; pi0 bijectivity plus pi1 isomorphism. Level 3 is essential surjectivity on action objects. In hyperbolic types the ambient identity group is contractible, so this is the manuscript's four cumulative tests. The coefficient-ladder comparison uses the precise constant, fully faithful, and equivalence tests on maps of 1-types.

C.4 describes coherent actions through the automorphism 2-group, including its H3 obstruction and the vanishing k-invariant for split abelian models. Pullback along EΓ -> BΓ explicitly rectifies a coherent action on a space of the same homotopy type. It does not turn that general space into the original surface. C.8 proves the finite hyperbolic map A^Γ -> Map_Γ(EΓ,A) is a weak equivalence, and a homotopy equivalence in CW models. C.9 computes the four contractible components for the torus action -I. C.10 proves the comparison for every finite action on the plane, annulus, and torus. It explicitly computes their peripheral-diagram 2-groups, including the plane peripheral circle and both annulus end labels.

## C(b): finite hyperbolic realization and the infinite tests

The four finite hyperbolic levels are proved in C.5–C.8.

* **Existence:** the original Kerckhoff paper was opened. Theorem 5 and Section IV explicitly cover cusps, and its mapping-class convention includes orientation reversal. The unique hyperbolic isometry in each realized class gives the actual homomorphism.
* **Uniqueness:** finite stabilizers have common invariant linear discs, using the checked Kolev theorem and B.1. The quotient has cone, mirror, and corner charts and admits a compatible smooth orbifold structure. An invariant conformal structure follows. Finite-type extremal-map uniqueness gives the exact conjugator, including anticonformal operators.
* **Connectedness with mirrors:** the canonical identity lift induces identity on the whole NEC lift group. The proof uses the trivial centralizer of the non-elementary surface subgroup, replacing the false assertion that individual reflections have cyclic centralizer. The lift extends as identity on the ideal boundary, hence preserves mirror chambers. This checks the quotient-orientation convention before applying orbifold Dehn–Nielsen–Baer injectivity.
* **Contractibility:** descent identifies the equivariant identity group with the quotient identity homeomorphism group, retaining singular, corner, and puncture marks. Every exception in Yagasaki's actual Lemma 2.1 list is excluded by negative orbifold Euler characteristic. Isotopies and parameter families lift through the finite quotient charts.

The source audit does not endorse unrelated assertions in Kolbe–Evans: their individual-centralizer sentence is repaired, and their claim that Mostow rigidity forces trivial outer automorphism groups is unused.

For the exceptional aspherical surfaces, C.10 proves that inclusion of the affine centralizer is a weak equivalence. Integrating a periodic displacement extracts a commuting affine map continuously. The residual zero-mean factor is contractible: after quotienting translations, a rotation fixed set allows reduction to C.7 by puncturing; the remaining reflection quotients are an annulus, Möbius band, Klein bottle, strip, half-open annulus, or punctured projective plane, with the precise Yagasaki homotopy types. The mean map has a translation section, so its map on the cyclic fundamental group is an isomorphism. Plane cone and sector quotients give the corresponding centralizer calculations directly. Finally, averaging real cochains identifies H¹(Γ,T) with H²(Γ,Λ), and the exact fixed-point sequence identifies the actual and derived fixed components and fundamental groups. This closes the parametric gap that ordinary finite-action normal forms alone would leave.

For infinite groups, C.11 constructs a Z-action by two local radial rotations. A supported half-turn centralizes the action and exchanges its two isolated fixed points, so it lies in another centralizer component. The same nonidentity action and the trivial action induce the same mapping-class homomorphism and cannot be conjugate. These prove failures at levels 1 and 2. C.23 proves level 3 failure for Γ=Mod^+(X_g), g>=2, by exact reduction to the checked Markovic–Saric nonsection theorem. C.12 does not claim an example at level 0.

## C(c): the critical-configuration argument

C.13 proves both (B/B^0)^Γ=1 and H^1(Γ,B^0)=1 for faithful positive finite hyperbolic actions. An invariant braid class has an equivariant representative by uniqueness on the punctured surface; contractibility on the filled surface makes its equivariant isotopy fix every point of the finite critical set. A 1-cocycle twists the action into a second action on the punctured surface, and marked conjugacy makes it a coboundary. The component group is trivial, with exact descent to Homeo(V/Γ)^0.

Keeping the same finite configuration for reflection groups is refuted in C.14. Complex conjugation on the three-punctured sphere fixes three open arcs. No finite deletion removes them, and a remaining fixed point gives torsion in the lift extension. Scans 282–286 already begin with Γ=Γ^+. The full centralizer theorem for reflections is C.7; its quotient retains mirror strata.

## C(d): drilling and the torsion-free extension theorem

C.15 proves the discrete faithful positive drilling equivalence, the general Phi disjoint-union bijection, full faithfulness of filling, and uniqueness up to the specified exterior isomorphism. The comparison homomorphism kills the normal closure of selected new peripheral subgroups. Phi is identified with critical points by unique fixed points of finite positive hyperbolic isometry groups. The geometric realization and isotopy inputs are isolated.

Two omitted hypotheses in unrestricted drilling are essential. C.14 treats reflections. C.16 treats nonfaithful outer actions: for the trivial C2 action on F2 of type (0,3), realization, filling, and finite-order rigidity force the drilled action to be trivial, so its centre-free extension is a product with C2 and has torsion.

C.17 proves the torsion-free finite extension theorem allowing nonorientable quotient surfaces. Torsion-freeness first forces faithfulness. Realization gives a free finite action, whose quotient has fundamental group E. End stabilizers preserve orientation, since a local reflection would fix a ray in the punctured neighbourhood. Thus the upstairs cusp has finite index in a downstairs infinite cyclic cusp equal to its centralizer. Relative PD2 is proved from compact truncation and cap-product chains; it is not inferred from virtual freeness alone.

C.18 proves the discrete positive criterion of scan294 in both directions by exact covering and filling maps. Mirrors require boundary-stratum data beyond finite puncture loops. The profinite analogue belongs to Part D. D.13–D.14 were independently checked: their orientation and primitive peripheral-closure comparisons match C.17.

## C(e): topology of SA, correction, and extension classes

C.19 refutes the subspace compact-open interpretation of the special homeomorphism theorem. A boundary twist supported in s/3<|z|<2s/3 has uniform displacement at most 4s/3 and tends to the identity. Each map remains identity near the puncture. The special annulus subgroup is consequently path connected in this topology, whereas the framed group is Z. The formula is both proved and checked numerically.

C.20 supplies the fixed-compact-boundary formulation. Boundary restriction gives the central Z^nu extension by positive twists and a contractible identity component. Its class is the vector of tangent-circle Euler classes, the negatives of cotangent psi classes in the usual complex convention. The annulus has a diagonal rotation relation; the disc kernel is zero.

C.21 computes the four classes. They vanish for (0,3) and (0,4). For (1,1), e generates H²(SL2Z,Z)=Z/12: the C4*_{C2}C6 decomposition and finite circle rotations give restrictions (1,1). Translation identifies the tangent lines on a two-pointed elliptic curve, so the vector for (1,2) is (q*e,q*e). Order-four and order-three marked rotations detect order twelve. This avoids an unexplained B3 presentation. C.22 proves nonsplitting over every nontrivial finite positive puncture stabilizer by the extension Z -> Z -> C_n.

## E(a): special groups

E.1 defines the conjugator pairs and computes multiplication, inverses, the canonical normal inner subgroup, and product kernel. For hyperbolic discrete groups and full profinite completions, SE -> Γ has kernel T^I × pi. Closedness and continuity are checked directly. The exclamation variant is the inverse image of the trivial permutation on I.

E.2 gives inverse constructions between pairs and framed-diagram autoequivalences. Gauging the first square removes peripheral natural-transformation ambiguity; the remaining generic basepoint change is exactly the inner subgroup. E.3 proves the puncture pushout by [(alpha,(g_j))] -> Int(g_i) alpha, with gauge independence, multiplication, image, and kernel checked. E.4 gives exact exceptional failures: the torus has no conjugator kernel or injective inner copy, the annulus inner subgroup is diagonal inside T², and the plane peripheral map is noninjective.

## Evidence, prior returns, and files

LM_P027–LM_P031 transcriptions were read alongside actual scans at 281, 286, 293, 297, and 304. Mathematical support for ambiguous readings is recorded without silent choices. ## Continuation: the sphere and the tube action

C.24 refutes the integral fixed-to-homotopy-fixed assertion for the unpunctured sphere by a faithful order-three rotation. Its identity-centralizer loop group is Z, while the derived fixed-point loop group is Z_3 times C2; the comparison is n -> (n,n modulo 2). We checked the precise p-completed mapping-space homotopy formula in Jackowski–McClure–Oliver, *Maps between classifying spaces revisited*, Theorem 1.9 and its proof. Its arithmetic-fracture reduction is written out integrally. The missing element beta=sum 3^(j!), paired with 0 in C2, supplies an explicit failure of surjectivity. `verify_sphere_comparison.py` checks the exact order-three matrix action, commutation equations and finite CRT reductions. No finite reduction is represented as proving the inverse-limit obstruction.

C.12 now includes the exact universal-cover centralizer comparison supplied by the independent Part A analysis. It proves that all centralizer loops lift closed and isolates both failed assertions in geodesic interpolation: equivariance for arbitrary lift groups and preservation of the homeomorphism property. This strengthens the attack without claiming a zero-level result.

B.30–B.32 in `b_extra.tex` complete the formerly missing tube-and-strip computations. A two-vertex cellular presentation gives exact words for r,s,tau,rho_j; the inner factors caused by moving the pole basepoint are explicit. `verify_tube_action.py` checks relator images and integral homology for g=2,...,8. `check_tube_dehn.py` additionally verifies all finite-factor and tube-conjugacy relations in the actual surface groups by their C'(1/6) Dehn reductions. `verify_tube_local.py` verifies the eight C2^3 tube maps and the exact coordinate change to the manuscript antipodal formula. Finally the mod-two quadratic-refinement obstruction proves that one selected one-holed-torus subgroup per tube never generates the full positive mapping class group, even when the strips overlap. All possible transversal mod-two intersection matrices are exhaustively checked for g=2,3,4 by `verify_tube_action.py`; the paper proves the unrestricted identity.

The manuscript scans 467,469,470 were inspected directly against the transcription. The margin of scan 467 already anticipates a central extension of SL2Z, matching the proved B3 quotient. The uncertain GP/GL lettering is left undecided.

C.25 supplies a faithful infinite cyclic sphere action with failure of level 0 for the fundamental-groupoid transporter comparison. An irrational axis rotation has actual identity centralizer of homotopy type S1; the weak transporter in Pi1(Homeo(S2)) has loop group C2. The actual map is reduction modulo two, with kernel 2Z, proved by the exact latitude parametrization and quaternion covering. This answers the requested existence of an infinite-group zero-level failure without changing C.12's stronger hyperbolic-surface question. C.24 remains the untruncated homotopy-fixed computation and is explicitly distinguished by the comparison maps.

Independent audit of B.18–B.23: the centralizer sequence follows by adjusting an automorphism lift by the displayed inner conjugator; boundary permutations lift positively in both real quotient families. The cut-system normalizer sequence accounts for independent tube-end swaps via the sphere group preserving unordered pairs. ## D.30: closed genus-two profinite counterexample

The inverse-pair construction extends to two punctured tori. The exact full profinite boundary-power automorphism is verified in Nakamura, PRIMS49(3)(2013),413–496, Section2.5p424; the source explicitly allows all finite groups and stabilizes the chosen inertia through the cyclotomic character. `d30.tex` defines the glued automorphism directly on the standard closed genus-two presentation, proves its square is the named inner automorphism, and builds the index-two extension by an explicit continuous group law. A direct presentation elimination identifies the maximal pro-2 quotient with the closed nonorientable genus-three group. The infinite Demushkin cohomological-dimension result (Wilkes Section1.4 and Theorem1.9) proves that quotient torsion-free; because quotient parity factors through it, all torsion in the full extension is excluded.

The orientation multiplier follows from the Heisenberg determinant identity and the natural surface intersection pairing. The odd-prime restriction maps identify H²(E,F3)=F3 and H²(E,F5)=0, excluding every discrete surface completion. The maximal pro-3 quotient is Z3², and every other odd maximal pro-p quotient is free of rank2; these comparisons follow by both universal properties. B independently executed all free-word, parity, matrix and finite cochain certificates in `code/part_b/closed_extension_d30_audit.py`. The proof and metadata are `d30.tex`, `d30_result.json`, and `d30_references.bib`; no root file was edited.

## D.32: finite pure three-cusp loop groups

The third cusp closes the fixed-subgroup gap left after D.28. The new closed-subgroup lemma proves that a closed C in Fhat2 with dim H1(C,Fp)=2 and containing conjugates of the full Zp parts of all three pants cusps must equal Fhat2. The restriction colimit over open neighborhoods is proved by extension of finite characters. Finite p-part of infinite index would give unbounded Schreier dimensions under injective restrictions. Infinite p-part supplies a finite cover of p-divisible degree in which the three selected boundary degrees are prime to p, forcing an additional boundary and three independent restricted characters. The exact boundary restriction scalar is d_i inverse modulo p.

The normalized lift of the entire pure loop-outer group is constructed with explicit inner conjugators; its image is the Belyi group, and no identification with its GT0 subgroup is assumed. D.28's section and Brown argument uses only these shared properties. The result rules out every prime-order element whose p-adic multiplier is one. Every finite pure loop subgroup therefore injects into C2 by its 2-adic multiplier. Every finite full type-(0,3) loop subgroup injects into S3 times C2 by the actual pair of permutation and multiplier maps. Conjugacy of the actions and mixed odd-prime signs of the surviving involutions remain explicit open portions of the larger question.

`three_cusp_rank_d32.py` executed successfully. Every connected pants cover through degree six is represented by running x through cycle types and y through all permutations. The checks include 18,156 boundary-homology computations and 31,398 selected-triple restriction computations at p=2,3,5, with explicit dual characters and evaluation matrices saved in JSON. `ls2_lemma9_audit.tex` verifies the source's fixed-f multiplier assertion by Heisenberg and dihedral quotient maps; it does not infer a torsion statement from the equation for the square of the multiplier alone.

## D.33: finite punctured-torus actions and primary homology

The cohomology-ring refinement gives a unique section class whenever an order-p automorphism of Fhat_r acts trivially on its maximal pro-p quotient. The precise map E→F^(p)×Cp induces a mod-p cohomology ring isomorphism by Hochschild-Serre comparison. Multiplication of the r ambient characters by a positive power of the periodic Cp class remains injective. Under Brown restriction these classes lie in the first cohomology of the actual centralizers, forcing the sum of their dimensions to be at least r. The exact Brown dimension formula r+1 forces one section class and makes restriction H1(F,Fp)→H1(C,Fp) an isomorphism.

For the punctured torus, the peripheral normalizer contains a section by the exact norm calculation, so C contains the full Zp part of a conjugate commutator cusp. The cohomology restriction isomorphism makes that element invisible to every character of C. A proper C has infinite p-index, and a finite cover of p-divisible degree then supplies a nonzero boundary character, with the exact same d inverse restriction scalar as D32. This contradiction proves C=F. D27 supplies the actual finite-order lift; its p-adic homology being trivial makes its induced pro-p automorphism trivial without any further conjugation.

Thus every finite p-subgroup of the full type-(1,1) loop-outer group injects into GL2(Zp), and full abelianization is faithful on each finite subgroup. An explicit local matrix argument bounds the order of every finite subgroup by a divisor of24. The proof retains the remaining action classification and conjugacy questions. `torus_homology_d33.py` executed all finite-cover boundary calculations through degree six, the characteristic-two Magnus nonpower test, and periodic cohomology multiplication certificates. The complete fragment, metadata and executed output are `d33.tex`, `d33_result.json`, and the matching code/JSON/stdout files.

## D.34: global sign of every pure involution

All odd-prime signs are now settled for the entire pure three-cusp loop group. First, a pure involution on a genus-zero n-cusp full profinite group, with 2-adic multiplier minus one, has a canonical n-cycle of section classes. Each peripheral normalizer splits, with exactly two local section classes. Their mod-two abelianization difference is the corresponding cusp vector. Brown bounds all section classes by n; a forest with n edges would require n+1 vertices, and every cycle must use all cusp edges because their only nonzero binary relation is their total sum. Thus exactly n section classes form the n-cycle, and all their centralizers have zero first mod-two cohomology.

A plus sign at an odd prime q makes the normalized pants involution pure on the explicit cyclic cover ker(Fhat2→Cq, x→1,y→0), a sphere with q+2 cusps. Its extension is normal in the original extension because E/N=Cq×C2. Conjugation by x gives an order-q action on kernel-conjugacy section classes, fixing two cusp edges and cycling the other q. The cycle graph has automorphism group of order2(q+2), which excludes that action. Therefore every odd-prime sign is minus one. The remaining pure involution question is conjugacy to complex conjugation, with no multiplier ambiguity left.

The triangle also proves f is an actual alpha-coboundary h inverse alpha(h), with h in F′. The two elements x and h y h inverse are inverted and give automorphisms in every pro-p quotient. Full generation and preservation of the third cusp by that endomorphism are not inferred. `pure_involution_d34.py` executed 60 exact normalizer-square parameter checks, all binary cusp relations for n=3 through12, and cyclic-cover/deck obstructions at q=3,5,7,11,13,17,19. The complete proof is `d34.tex`, with metadata and JSON/stdout certificates alongside the code.

## D.36: global sign of a positive transposition

Every full pants involution with positive 2-adic multiplier has a transposition permutation and globally positive multiplier. The proof first extends D33's actual finite-order automorphism argument to a genus-zero group with n≥3 cusps, using the same all-n-cusp finite-cover rank test. For a transposition fixing x and exchanging y,z, a hypothetical negative sign at an odd q makes the quotient character (x,y,z)→(0,1,−1) invariant. Its planar q-fold cover has q x-cusps and one y- and z-cusp. The involution's translation on the q x-cusps has order2 and must be trivial. Filling the exchanged y,z lifts gives a pure actual sphere-q involution, which the positive-2-adic lemma excludes.

The covering and filling maps are explicit Schreier substitutions: a_i=y^i x y^−i, b=y^q, d=z^q=b^−1 a_(q−1)^−1⋯a_0^−1. Killing b,d leaves the presentation a_0⋯a_(q−1)=1. `positive_transposition_d36.py` executed these free-word identities and both filling maps, together with the cyclic quotient and involutive translation checks, at q=3,5,7,11,13,17,19. The global orientation characters follow for positive S3, V4 with pure kernel, and full S3×C2 subgroups. Negative transpositions and order-three multipliers remain distinct unresolved portions of the full-pants action question.

A negative transposition has a peripheral-normalizer section by the coordinate norm calculation. Its mod-two homology is the regular C2 module, so Hochschild–Serre and full profinite Brown restriction give one section class. The diagonal line in the V4 quotient therefore has torsion-free preimage and gives the required four-cusp double transposition. Conversely the center-free V4 extension containing this specified deck group identifies its deck preimage with the original Fhat2 and descends the actual peripheral powers; multiplication by two being injective fixes the descended exponent. These are inverse marked-extension constructions.

The two commuting single transpositions both exchange the two pants partitions that D29’s double transposition exchanges. Thus an extra geometric deck symmetry must move the chosen separator. The exact limit is illustrated by a geometric specialization: one transported standard deck fails outer commutation in an explicit S3 quotient, while a second explicit free-word deck commutes up to the named inner automorphism and moves the separator. All covering changes, free extension eliminations, deck squares, commutation identities, periodic cochain matrices and 96 relabeled partition checks passed in negative_transposition_d37.py, with JSON/stdout retained. ## D.41: every negative pants transposition has global multiplier minus one

The last involution multiplier ambiguity is resolved. A least odd prime q with positive multiplier would make the cyclic cover with monodromies (-2,1,1) invariant. Filling its unique y,z cusps gives a genus (q−1)/2 surface with one boundary. The full deck extension is explicitly Cq∐Cq, mapping both generators to1 in Cq; the kernel has cyclically permuted Schreier generators u_i and the single relation product u_i=1. Its homology is exactly the multiplicity-one cyclotomic module Qp[X]/Phi_q.

The induced involution commutes with this deck action and is a symplectic similitude with its original multiplier, by the exact degree-two boundary-word calculation. A prime divisor p of q−1 has even order modulo q: otherwise their product −1 would have odd order in Fq*. For that p, each local cyclotomic factor is invariant under inversion, so its symplectic restriction is nondegenerate and the commuting involution is a scalar sign on the entire factor. Its similitude multiplier is therefore plus one at p, contradicting p<q and the choice of q (including the prescribed negative sign at2).

The complete proof and metadata are d41.tex and d41_result.json. negative_multiplier_d41.py executes the exact free-product Schreier/boundary identities, unimodular boundary form and deck-module identities for thirteen odd primes through43, and the prime-factor/Frobenius calculation for all302 odd primes below2000. The all-prime result is proved in the text. Together with D34 and D36, this determines the global multiplier of every involution and every finite subgroup generated by involutions, without asserting conjugacy.

Each nontrivial central-character summand has odd dimensionq². An involution that inverts the central character at one odd prime exchanges two nondegenerately paired odd-dimensional summands. Taking determinants of the actual alternating-pairing equation forces the negative multiplier at every prime.

For a positive3-adic multiplier, the action on the Heisenberg group of order27 is conjugated, through an explicit inner conjugation of the actual automorphism, to identity or simultaneous inversion. Over the unramified quadratic field Q2(zeta3), the two spaces are Galois paired, and each multiplicity matrix has determinant plus or minus one. The determinant of the pairing equation forces the2-adic multiplier positive. These two arguments give the global sign of every torus involution.

## D.46: three positive involution sections and actual order-four lifting

For a positive torus involution, D33 excludes the identity 2-adic homology action; its matrix is therefore minus the identity. In its full semidirect extension the two extended mod-two characters satisfy chi squared equals u times chi. The displayed mod-four one-cochain proves this Bockstein identity exactly. After inverting u, the rank-two Hochschild–Serre calculation gives a three-dimensional algebra generated by idempotents. This algebra is reduced. Full Brown restriction identifies it with a product of the section-normalizer cohomology algebras; each nonzero first cohomology of a section centralizer would supply a square-zero element. Thus there are exactly three full section classes, all with vanishing first mod-two centralizer cohomology.

An outer order-four action permutes those three classes through its quotient of order two and fixes one class. Its section normalizer surjects onto C4. Dividing by the central involution gives an extension of a projective closed subgroup of Fhat2 by C2 whose maximal pro-two quotient is exactly C2. If this quotient group were torsion-free, Serre's finite-index dimension theorem would make it projective, contradicting its nonfree maximal pro-two quotient. A torsion element consequently gives the required order-four section with its prescribed label.

The completed proof and metadata are d46.tex and d46_result.json. torus_order4_lift_d46.py executed all 16,384 mod-four cochain pair comparisons and their explicit cup-square coboundary comparison, the Boolean-algebra evaluation map, and every three-dimensional quotient. JSON and stdout are saved. The new conclusion is actual order-four lifting; it does not claim geometric conjugacy or resolve the remaining mixed signs of an order-four multiplier.

## D.48: two order-three sections and the peripheral normalizer

The order-three torus action has 3-adic characteristic polynomial Phi3 by D33, and its coinvariant abelian group has order three. Its full semidirect extension therefore has maximal pro-three abelian quotient C3 squared. No nonzero mod-three character lifts to Z/9. The coefficient Bockstein is consequently injective on its two-dimensional first cohomology. This forces both degree-two spectral-sequence terms to survive; the explicit norm-zero cyclic resolution gives periodicity starting even at its degree-zero row-one term. All higher differentials vanish and both positive-degree cohomology dimensions remain two.

If there were one section class, the Bockstein of the character vanishing on that section would restrict to zero on its normalizer, because it is pulled back from a closed subgroup of cohomological dimension at most one. Its nonzero periodic multiples contradict full Brown restriction. Hence there are exactly two full section classes and both centralizers have zero first mod-three cohomology. A finite covering boundary character then prevents either centralizer from containing the full cusp Z3. This makes the cusp normalizer torsion-free. The exact peripheral norm equation shows that the cube of any labeled generator lift has unit 3-adic cusp exponent.

The proof and metadata are d48.tex and d48_result.json. torus_order3_sections_d48.py executed all 59,049 finite semidirect cochain pairs, all abelian character checks, and the integral/mod-three matrix and cyclic-resolution comparisons. JSON and stdout are saved. A's independent proof audit and rerun passed. The global order-three multiplier and geometric conjugacy remain unresolved.

## D.50: exact abstract finite two-subgroup types

The faithful primary homology map of D33 reduces the finite two-subgroup calculation to matrices over Q2 of order at most eight. An order-four matrix has square minus the identity and centralizer Q2(i). That field contains at most four roots of two-power order. Thus an order-eight group has a cyclic index-two centralizer and is dihedral or quaternion. In the quaternion case the anticommuting matrix has entries (a,b;b,-a) and requires a squared plus b squared to equal minus one. Minimum 2-adic valuation, followed by the exact unit-square residues modulo eight and four, excludes this equation.

The resulting abstract types are 1, C2, C4, V4, and D8. Explicit maps r(x)=y, r(y)=x inverse, s(x)=y, s(y)=x realize D8, preserve the full commutator cusp with the displayed conjugators, and realize all five types through named subgroups. D43 determines the full multipliers on every V4 and D8 subgroup: the rotation subgroup is positive, and the reflections are negative. An arbitrary cyclic C4 retains the exact global-square and positive-2-coordinate restrictions, without a global sign or conjugacy assertion. The proof and metadata are d50.tex and d50_result.json. The executed torus_two_subgroups_d50.py verifies all generator-word identities, eight distinct homology matrices, and 48 unit-residue square comparisons; output and stdout are saved.

## D.53: one labelled order-four section class

D46's three positive-square section classes are distinguished by three points of the actual affine mod-two quotient. The order-four homology matrix has nonidentity reduction, so its action transposes two points and fixes one. For an actual lift b, the normalizer of T=<b squared> is C semidirect C4 with C=C_F(T), and its exact quotient by T is C semidirect C2. Projectivity, cd2 at most one, and zero H1(C,F2) make that full quotient's cohomology equal to the cohomology of C2. The inspected Brown theorem then gives exactly one quotient involution class and zero first fixed-group cohomology.

Every order-four section has its square in the unique invariant class. Moving that square to T puts the section inside the normalizer, and the unique quotient involution moves it to the exact inverse image <b>. The kernel of this last quotient is the specified group T of order two, so that inverse image has order four. All conjugators lie in F and preserve the original generator label. The normalizer of <b> is C_F(b) times C4, with H1(C_F(b),F2)=0. There are exactly two involution classes in E and precisely one lies in C4. Files d53.tex and d53_result.json give the full proof; torus_order4_sections_d53.py executes the mod-four obstruction and all twelve invariant affine three-point configurations. The result is a section-class theorem for the specified extension and makes no outer geometric conjugacy assertion.

## D.54 and D.55: prime-by-prime faithfulness and the exact nine finite abstract types

The labelled torsion-free cusp normalizer gives a root v with v cubed equal to a primitive three-adic boundary power for an order-three action. An invariant character to Cq, q different from three, would put this same v in the exact semidirect extension of the type-(1,q) covering kernel. Deck averaging over Z3 identifies the invariant homology with the original torus homology through the index-q lattice inclusion; its complementary summand is the boundary augmentation. The cube norm vanishes modulo three on both summands, but the primitive boundary power does not. This excludes every such invariant character, makes the two-adic representation faithful on every finite subgroup, and removes C12 and D24 from D52's allowed list.

For an arbitrary positive involution, D46 supplies zero first mod-two cohomology for every section centralizer. The full closed-subgroup boundary-character argument then excludes a split cusp normalizer. Its labelled square exponent must be a two-adic unit by the exact full norm equation. Applying the same type-(1,q) deck-averaging contradiction over Z2 proves that every odd-primary homology matrix is minus the identity; the two-adic statement was already proved. Thus every primary homology map is faithful on every finite torus subgroup. The explicit Q3(zeta3) normalizer from D52 excludes Dic12.

Consequently the occurring finite abstract subgroup types are exactly 1, C2, C3, C4, V4, C6, S3, D8 of order eight, and D12 of order twelve. All nine have the named geometric realizations already supplied in D50/D52. The proofs and metadata are d54.tex, d54_result.json, d55.tex, and d55_result.json. A nontrivial cubic multiplier must have scalar homology lambda squared at its bad coordinate; a negative quartic coordinate must have scalar homology plus or minus i at an odd prime congruent to one modulo four. Those cyclic multiplier cases and geometric conjugacy remain unresolved.

## D.58: negative involution sections and exact cusp fusion
