### F.15: the full two-point fixed subgroup and every correcting coset

Retain F.12's actual six-puncture sphere M, based pair (1,c0), and
involution r(z)=c0/z. The two-point configuration involution sends
(x,y) to (r(y),r(x)). Its based group J is the actual two-point
pushing kernel in the pure eight-point mapping class group. The
proof identifies its full completion with that ambient kernel,
using the split free-by-free Fadell--Neuwirth sequence and the two
actual completed Birman sequences. The two-track quotient is the
product of the two based Fhat5 groups. Its kernel is exactly
B=K0 intersect K2 from F.14.

Removing the two unmarked fixed points of r from M gives the
fixed-configuration map z -> (z,r(z)). Its double-cover monodromy
identifies the resulting F7 with the discrete fixed subgroup of J.
Full induced topology follows through the exact point-pushing and
prime-order normalizer maps. On the two-track quotient its map is
the graph of the filling epimorphism F7 -> F5. In the completion
the fixed subgroup is this actual Fhat7, and its intersection with B
is the kernel of that filling, the closed normal closure of the two
specified branch meridians.

The section comparison retains the original labels. Punctured
Nielsen realization and the double-cover monodromy give a pure
conjugator between any discrete order-two section and the specified
one. Independently correcting each within-pair choice retains all
eight labels. The surjective map from the pure eight-point centralizer
to the pure six-point centralizer follows by paired point motion on
M minus the two branch points. Removing its exact lift from the
conjugator gives a conjugator in J. The sections therefore have one
labelled J-conjugacy class.

For E=J semidirect C2, the proof verifies residual finiteness,
goodness, finite virtual cohomological type and the comparison for
all discrete F2[[Ehat]]-modules required by Boggi--Zalesskii Theorem A.
A finite graph-of-graphs model, finite-type resolutions and filtered
unions of finite invariant submodules provide the required module
comparison. The theorem's prime-order conjugacy and centralizer
statements then give the completed section comparison and the exact
fixed subgroup. The argument uses these proved prime-order statements
with their hypotheses checked for E.

Every B-valued involution cocycle is consequently a B-coboundary.
First correct it in Jhat; the projection of that correcting element
is in the fixed graph of the two-track quotient. Surjectivity of the
fixed Fhat7 onto that graph removes the projection and places the
correction in B. Equal coboundaries correspond exactly to left cosets
of C_B(r). The coset parametrization is a homeomorphism by compactness.
The same computation gives the exact surjective fixed-quotient sequence
from C_P8(r) to (P8/B)^r, with kernel C_B(r).

For each u in the seven-point equalizer E7, the defect
phi(u) inverse times R(phi(u)) therefore has a nonempty correcting
coset in B. The paper gives the exact centralizing coset of phi(u)
and its ambiguity C_B(r). The original curve image meets B trivially
by F.14, so it contributes at most one element to that coset. That
element is its original representative precisely for u in the full
equalizer. Proving that this full equalizer is trivial remains the
explicit F.7 obligation. No correction outside the original curve
image is asserted to solve that equation.

The primary sources Boggi--Zalesskii, arXiv:2406.08639v2,
Definitions 1.1--1.2 and Theorem A(i),(iii), and Kerckhoff (1983),
Theorem 4 and Section IV, printed pages 255--256, were reopened.
Both are existing verified bibliography entries. F.15 introduces
no finite computation used to infer a full profinite conclusion;
the existing executed F.12/F.14 certificates retain the exact
basepoint, labels, involution and forgetful formulas. The full root
audit and independent B audit are attached.


# Six-claim continuation: relative fillings, the quartic multiplier and exact forgetting ambiguity

This continuation incorporates C68--C70, D69--D70 and F16 after independent proof review. The return remains INCOMPLETE: C12, D12 and F7 retain the exact unresolved statements specified below. These six results do not change that status.

## C68: the actual spherical quotient and first-kind boundary comparison

C68 extends the prime-end comparison of C67 to a simply connected spherical domain whose frontier is a nondegenerate locally connected continuum. The actual closed-disk quotient is retained, including distinct prime ends with the same frontier value. The proof excludes alternating distinct boundary fibres by the Jordan separation argument and derives rigidity of the induced circle map. It then constructs both inverse homomorphisms between the boundary-fixed disk group and the ambient group fixing the domain complement, and proves their topology comparison. This includes unbounded plane domains with the specified infinity in the fixed complement.

The equivariant assertion retains a specified closed-disk conjugacy and a torsion-free first-kind Fuchsian subgroup in the actual proper interior group. Tukia's extension theorem applies to the given interior homeomorphism. Its boundary map fixes the dense hyperbolic fixed-point set. Restriction is therefore an isomorphism of the two closed Polish centralizers, so its continuous inverse extends an entire compact parameter family. The original operator labels and any restriction kernel are retained throughout.

The original Tukia paper was opened and checked at Definition 3.2, Lemma 3.4, Proposition 3.5 and Corollary 3.5.1, printed pp.30--32. The primary article is Pekka Tukia, *On discrete groups of the unit disk and their isomorphisms*, Annales Academiae Scientiarum Fennicae, Series A I, no.504 (1972), pp.1--45, DOI 10.5186/aasfm.1972.504. Section 3.B supplies the extension used here without a finite-generation or cocompactness assumption. The stronger hypotheses of the subsequent section are not substituted. The bibliography entry C_Tukia1972 records this primary-source verification.

## C69: Fuchsian actions of either kind and all quotient boundary components

C69 proves the relative centralizer filling for every specified closed-disk conjugate of a torsion-free discrete Fuchsian action. The trivial, parabolic cyclic and hyperbolic cyclic actions are included. The actual partial compactification is D union Omega, where Omega is the ordinary set on the original boundary circle. The explicit Mobius convergence formula proves that its quotient is a regular universal covering of a second-countable surface with boundary.

For a commuting homeomorphism of the partial compactification fixing Omega, controlled axis accesses give the same endpoints under the map and its inverse. A third controlled point determines which complementary cap is carried to which cap. This proves extension across the entire limit set. The continuous bijective restriction homomorphism between closed Polish centralizers then gives the full parameter-family extension, including inverse families.

The quotient filling uses the pointed identity-component theorem of Yagasaki with every exception checked. The original arXiv:math/0010223 PDF was reopened at Theorem 1.1, Corollary 1.1 and Lemma 2.1. The quotient's nontrivial infinite fundamental group excludes the pointed exceptions. A separate explicit proper-collar construction corrects an ambient parameter-disk filling to fix every boundary component. Circle lifts are normalized over the actual simply connected parameter disk; they are exactly identity on its parameter boundary. Properness reduces compact-set checks to finitely many line or circle components, even when the surface has infinitely many of them. The resulting lift retains each original deck label and recovers the original loop exactly.

This proves the stated closed-disk-conjugacy case. It does not establish extension of an arbitrary interior uniformizing conjugacy to a prescribed prime-end circle, or simultaneous pasting across infinitely many unrelated domains. Those remain part of C12.

## C70: compact-family evaluation carriers

C70 begins with the original compact labelled family from C64 and adjoins the loop and its inverse. For the resulting compact family P, the inclusion P(x) into the interior of P-squared(x) gives a controlled planar filled hull. Every interior component of that hull is simply connected. Intersecting the relevant component with the original open orbit gives a domain whose fundamental group injects into that of the orbit; the proof supplies the puncture choices and the explicit planar retraction needed for that direction.

The original null evaluation loop consequently has a filling in this carrier. Choosing one filling on each operator orbit and transporting it is well-defined: an operator fixing x fixes its entire generated orbit pointwise. This gives exact operator equivariance. Compactness of P-squared forces all carriers, and every chosen filling in them, to shrink uniformly at a fixed frontier point. The proof does not provide joint continuity as x varies or disjointness for simultaneous configuration fillings. Both requirements remain explicit in C12. The independent review requested one wording repair in the properness argument; the text now chooses a sequence escaping every compact set.

## D69: the integral action on the exact rotation cover

D69 starts with the actual finite dihedral quotient of D68 supplied by a negative odd quartic coordinate. Its kernel has type (q,2). The proof gives inverse integral graph-cycle maps, the full odd-deck decomposition and the exact dual homology convention. Brown restriction and the explicit mod-four character cochain determine the rotation image of the positive-square fixed subgroup: it is all of Cq. There are exactly two labelled section classes in the retained kernel extension, and the fixed-kernel first mod-two cohomology has dimension q-1.

The final integral strengthening uses an actual fixed lift of the rotation generator. The two Brown factors have square-zero positive fixed-group ideals, while the rotation group fixes their residue coordinates. Averaging identifies the nontrivial deck summand divided by the quotient class with that nilradical. The same cochain comparison gives identity modulo four for the positive square. Its actual order-two equation then gives identity over Z2. The action on the averaged rank-three part remains the explicitly retained minus-transpose deck matrix.

The producer torus_quartic_bad_prime_d69.py executes 134,418 checks: eleven dihedral-cover models, inverse graph maps, section labels, cyclic cohomology dimensions, 65,536 mod-four cochain checks, 65,536 cup-square comparisons and the retained mod-four quartic matrices. Root and the independent reviewer reran it. These checks certify the formulas; the full profinite restriction and topology assertions are proved in the text.

## D70: exclusion of every negative quartic coordinate

D70 closes the quartic multiplier branch. Fill the two specified cusps of the D69 cover by the actual closed normal quotient, then take its maximal pro-two quotient. The cusp conjugators prove that the original automorphism descends. The result is the pro-two closed genus-q surface group, with character module identified exactly as the annihilator of the two original peripheral elements.

The odd deck projector is self-adjoint for the integral surface cup form. Its rank-two averaged summand and rank-2(q-1) complement form an orthogonal unimodular splitting. The positive square acts as minus identity on the first and identity on the second. It is therefore an actual nonidentity involution, trivial on mod-two first cohomology, with two fixed integral characters having nonzero reduced cup product.

Those characters extend to the actual cyclic semidirect product and kill every finite-order element. Each order-two section acts by an inner twist of the same involution and is still nonidentity on integral homology. Kochloukova--Zalesskii Corollary 2 gives a free pro-two fixed subgroup for every such section. On its exact direct-product normalizer, the cup of the two characters is zero. Theorem 2 is injective in degree three, so the product of that cup with the quotient class is zero globally. The multiplicative Hochschild--Serre sequence gives the opposite result: all mod-two first-cohomology classes extend, their products generate the surface class, and the actual section preserves the quotient class. Its nonzero row-two degree-three product survives. This contradiction excludes the chosen negative odd coordinate.

The author PDF Claus3.pdf was reopened at Corollary 2, Theorem 2 and the infinite-index/Demushkin proof. Every hypothesis and every twisted section is checked in D70. Only injectivity of restriction is used. Thus every order-four full torus element has global multiplier one, and every finite two-subgroup has its multiplier in the two global signs. No geometric conjugacy conclusion is inferred. The scalar cubic branch, arbitrary negative conjugacy, pants recognition and the remaining full low-type recognition stay in D12.

## F16: a full procyclic ambiguity under every proper forgetting

F16 uses the actual eight-point involution and its degree-two spherical quotient. The four named free orbit pairs become four ordinary labels; the two fixed points become the two branch labels. Push one branch label around each ordinary label along a fixed based free system. The square of each downstairs twist has the explicit pure lift (angle,t) maps to (angle+t,t) under the covering angle maps to twice angle. Its upstairs disk contains exactly the corresponding original pair.

Forgetting either point of that pair kills the lifted twist. The nested commutator of all four lifted twists is therefore killed by every individual and every proper labelled forgetting, and it centralizes the original involution. Its descended based free word has the exact unitriangular value I+16E15. The normalizer descent, finite-index pure image and full Birman topology transport this detector to the actual completed subgroup. Since multiplication by sixteen is injective on Zhat, the closure of the word is a full Zhat. F14's injective ordinary forgetting proves that this closure meets the original curve image trivially.

The exact pullback of all proper forgetting images consequently strictly contains that curve image, also after imposing common-involution centralization. Right multiplication by the same procyclic subgroup preserves every proper forgetting image inside each nonempty correcting centralizer coset. This proves the obstruction to that precise membership test; it supplies no nonidentity source-equalizer element. F7 remains unresolved at the original full source-membership equation.

The producer common_reflection_forgetting_ambiguity_f16.py and its output give 5,007 executed checks, including all eight individual and 255 proper word-forgetting substitutions, the annulus formulas, the integral matrix value and twenty-one finite primary reductions. Root and the independent reviewer reran it successfully. All producers, outputs, source fragments and written audits are included in the package.


# Six-claim continuation after the 225-claim checkpoint

This continuation adds C.71, C.72, D.71, D.72, F.17 and F.18. Each has a
complete proof, a claim-catalogue row, executed code and recorded output,
and a full independent audit in addition to the root review. The current
overviews C.12, D.12 and F.7 retain the precise unresolved statements.
The report remains INCOMPLETE.

## C.71: boundary control for the actual two-dimensional operator group

For a closed locally compact free proper operator group of dimension two,
the actual orbit homeomorphism identifies the disk interior with the
simply connected Lie group. The two Lie algebras give the additive plane
and the specified affine group. Evaluation identifies the interior
centralizer with right translations, including the inverse topology.

The crucial boundary comparison is proved for the given family. Its
fractional powers lie in the restriction of one compact set P cubed, by
the exact two- or three-letter path formula of C.37. Restriction of that
compact set to the dense disk interior is an injective continuous map
into a Hausdorff space, and hence has a continuous inverse on its image.
The entire logarithmic contraction therefore lifts with the original
boundary fixed. This proves that every based positive-dimensional sphere
family contracts. It does not replace the relative topology by an
unverified interior subgroup topology.

The same claim calculates a separate cyclic model using the coordinate
kappa(x)=x/(1+|x|) in each real direction. Its integer vertical translation
fixes the two full horizontal boundary edges. The exact fixed-set equation
excludes a closed-disk conjugacy to a cyclic Fuchsian generator. Horizontal
translation supplies a specific map on the retained partial strip that
does not extend relative to the full boundary. Nevertheless the full
boundary-relative centralizer is explicitly the group of continuous
families of increasing integer-equivariant real lifts whose displacements
vanish at both ends of the horizontal coordinate. The proof identifies
its full uniform topology and contracts it by interpolation. Its orbits
are exactly vertical lines. The producer passes 34,194 exact checks.

## C.72: the full local centralizer and a failed extension map

The spiral coordinate sends z to
z/(1+|z|) times exp(i log(1+|z|)). Its displayed inverse and a uniform
bounded-displacement estimate specify the original closed-disk action.
Four out-and-back paths consist of horizontal and vertical translations,
a periodic horizontal shear, and a compactly supported vertical shear.
Their extensions fix the boundary and generate a group transitive on the
interior. Any homeomorphism centralizing the whole generated group must
first be a translation; the two shears force its horizontal parameter to
be zero and its vertical parameter to be an integer. Thus the effective
full local centralizer is exactly the specified proper discrete cyclic
group, with no additional operator subgroup suppressed.

Every forward cyclic orbit accumulates at every boundary point, as proved
by rounding an explicitly chosen exponential sequence of integers. The
interior map (x,y) to (2x,y) commutes with this cyclic action. Two named
sequences approach the same arbitrary boundary point, while their images
approach points differing by the nontrivial phase exp(i log 2). The map
has no continuous extension at any boundary point.

The actual interior conjugacy to a parabolic Fuchsian action is also
specified, with inverse. It sends this same obstructed map to (u,v) to
(u,v squared) on the upper half-plane, which extends boundary-fixed with
its inverse. Consequently the conjugation isomorphism of the interior
centralizers does not identify the two actual relative subgroups. The
original loop contracts by scaling its four path amplitudes. The result
therefore addresses the exact extension comparison and supplies no
nonzero loop class for C.12. The producer passes 38,263 exact checks.

## D.71: the cubic cover, integral module and full section count

The proof begins with the actual invariant character of the cubic
action, normalized at the cusp-root section. An explicit cusp-preserving
free-basis change gives the cyclic covering basis. Its inclusion and
deck maps determine the integral homology action, retaining all three
cusp labels. At a remaining scalar coordinate q the displayed invariant
character gives the actual Frobenius quotient C_q semidirect C_3, and the
outer action on this quotient is proved to be the identity. The extension
quotient is therefore its actual direct product with the section C_3.

The covering has three cusps and genus (3q-1)/2. Explicit graph-cycle maps
and their inverses identify its integral homology with one trivial and
three regular rotation modules. The averaged rank-four cubic lattice is
free of rank two over the ramified quadratic three-adic ring. Filling the
three named cusps gives the actual closed-surface pro-three quotient;
the nontrivial rotation summand inherits a unimodular alternating form.

A fixed-cup argument is proved using the original Kochloukova--Zalesskii
fixed-subgroup theorem and Brown restriction theorem. Every relevant
section twist acts nontrivially on integral homology, so its fixed
pro-three subgroup is free. A nonzero cup of invariant integral characters
would yield a surviving highest-row cohomology class whose product with
the cyclic period contradicts Brown injectivity. The actual fixed pairing
is consequently divisible by three. On each rank-three rotation-character
block, its relation with the norm gives a unit norm coefficient and a
regular Jordan-three block. Residue-field descent and lifted cyclic
vectors prove the displayed integral free Z_3[C_3] module structure.

The full fixed subgroup's image in the finite rotation quotient is then
determined by the exact coset parametrization of section classes and the
high-degree cohomology bound. It maps onto C_q, and its cover kernel has
zero first cohomology with F_3 coefficients. These constraints have been
proved for the actual full maps. They neither exclude nor construct the
remaining scalar cubic action. The producer passes 13,078 exact checks.

## D.72: actual continuous cochains and higher defining systems

For every actual full pure pants involution, D.34 supplies three section
generators and the vanishing of first mod-two cohomology of their full
fixed subgroups. Each normalizer is identified by explicit inverse maps
with that fixed subgroup times its C_2 section. The one-row cohomology
comparison and the checked Brown theorem give the actual restriction
isomorphism in degrees above one. The displayed three-by-three character
matrix supplies the isomorphism in degree one; degree zero is the common
scalar field.

On normalized continuous cochains, evaluation on the three repeated
section tuples defines a strict unital differential graded algebra map
to F_2[t_0,t_1,t_2]/(t_i t_j for unequal indices), with zero differential.
The normalized cochains of C_2 have exactly this one-variable polynomial
description because the internal differential terms have an identity
argument and the two end terms cancel. Explicit indicator cochains on
the finite quotient F_2 cubed prove degreewise surjectivity. Its kernel is
therefore an acyclic differential graded ideal.

The proof recursively constructs every higher entry of a Massey defining
system inside that acyclic kernel. Neighboring cup vanishing puts the
first right-hand side in the kernel; the later right-hand sides remain
there and are cocycles by the cancellation of each triple product twice.
All degrees are positive and all cochains remain continuous. Including
the final entry makes the Massey value a coboundary. In degree one the
same recursion, including the explicit n=2 case, is exactly the matrix
equation for a continuous representation into U_(n+1)(F_2). It proves the
precise image of the superdiagonal map and prescribes the matrices at the
three actual sections. The image has F_(n+2) cubed character tuples.

The two restriction arrows give an actual DGA zigzag with the standard
involution's cochains. Pál--Quick's publisher article was opened and its
Definition 6.1 identifies the target Boolean graded algebra. No
real-projectivity hypothesis is asserted for the extension. The third
cusp and full conjugator equations of D.35 remain. The producer passes
38,177 exact checks, including an independent eight-state count.

## F.17: the exact domain of the rational branch drilling

The two rational new labels are a(s) and minus a(s), where
a(s)=(zeta-s)/(zeta+s). For each of the other four fifth roots u, the two
collision equations are s squared = u(2zeta-u) and
s squared = zeta u squared/(2u-zeta). The first four squared values are
distinct and outside the unit circle; the second four are their reciprocal
conjugates. This gives exactly sixteen additional source punctures, with
no collision at an original puncture and no simultaneous new collision.
The source filling maps have ranks 27 to 11 to 3, with named meridians.

Every new meridian fills to identity in the original rank-eleven source,
but its ten-point image is a named primitive two-point twist. An actual
global difference function on normalized moduli gives an integer winding
character with coefficient one, and completing that character detects
every profinite power. The literal rational drilling map consequently
cannot factor through the original source filling.

Forgetting four specified ordinary labels gives the six-point family
(0,infinity,1,a squared,a,minus a). Ordinary forgetting to four points
has parameter a squared and is an actual degree-two covering; it proves
full injectivity of this six-point source map. The involution quotient has
parameter s squared/zeta squared, with its separately displayed labelled
fibres and pure-sheet permutation. The proof establishes each comparison
through its own actual map and asserts no full injectivity of the entire
ten-point curve map. The producer passes 445 exact checks.

## F.18: a unique centralizing branch lift and the remaining coset

Before and after ordered drilling, the involution normalizers descend to
the same decorated six-point quotient. The deck element permutes old
labels, so the descent restricted to a pure centralizer has trivial
kernel. The unique pure lift gives an actual isomorphism from the full
ten-point centralizer to the branch-even subgroup of the full eight-point
centralizer. The two-point forgetting kernel thus has trivial fixed
subgroup. Its configuration presentation is explicitly F_8 by F_7;

coset calculation then gives precisely two full cocycle classes, both
with trivial fixed groups, distinguished by branch order.

For the original residual, the pure tau-descent injection first proves
pointwise centralization of beta. The original Möbius coordinate W gives
tau(W)=-W, beta(W)=a(s0)/W and tau beta(W)=-a(s0)/W. The two rational new
labels are therefore literally the beta and tau beta stabilizer orbits.
The direct V_4 centralizer comparison retains each, proving the residual's
branch character is zero. Every residual element consequently has its
unique full centralizing ordered branch lift ell(u).

For any source lift w of u, the element
b(w)=ell(u)^(-1) Phi_dagger(w) is the unique coboundary correction in the
actual two-point kernel J. Put K+=Phi_dagger(ker q). The exact filling
square and original source injection identify K+ with the intersection
of the rational drilling image and J. Replacing w by wv multiplies b(w)
on the right by Phi_dagger(v). Thus b(w)K+ defines a continuous map from
the residual to the compact right-coset space J/K+. Its distinguished
fibre is exactly the elements whose canonical branch lifts lie in the
rational drilling image. No normality of K+ is needed. Whether this fibre
is the whole residual, and whether the residual is trivial, are the

The six producers pass 124,191 exact checks in total. Every producer was

## C.73: the actual comparison for an arbitrary frontier

For a simply connected spherical domain O with nondegenerate frontier,
the actual conformal coordinate defines a homomorphism from the ambient
group fixing the complement of O to the boundary-fixed closed-disk group.
Every such ambient map fixes the entire prime-end circle. The proof first
shows that every prime-end arc contains accessible ends with distinct
landing values. Two accessible radial tails with a common landing value
give, after joining them in the interior, the actual Jordan separation
used to exclude interleaved landing fibers. A nonidentity orientation-
preserving circle action would violate that separation.

The original homomorphism is injective and continuous. Its graph is
closed by restriction to the dense interior, and the Polish group open
mapping theorem proves continuity in the stated direction. The displayed
compactly supported Alexander approximants, including their inverses,
prove that its image is dense. The locally connected case agrees with
the earlier actual quotient isomorphism.

For every non-locally-connected frontier, a nontrivial prime-end impression
gives two sequences with a common disk-end limit and distinct ambient
limits. Disjoint shrinking convex disks contain the paired points. The
explicit conical homeomorphisms on those disks and their inverses tend
uniformly to identity on the closed disk; their ambient conjugates fail
to do so. Pasting all the disk maps gives one boundary-fixed disk map
with no ambient relative extension. Thus the particular comparison has
proper dense image and a discontinuous inverse on its image in exactly
the claimed case. Every operator conjugation equation is retained.

The primary source checked for the crosscuts and landing facts is
Ortega–Ruiz del Portal, *Attractors with vanishing rotation number*, JEMS
13(6) (2011), 1569–1590, DOI 10.4171/JEMS/288, publisher PDF
https://ems.press/content/serial-article-files/31798 . The proof uses the
specified background facts inside the proof of Lemma 7, before its
additional dynamical assumptions. Binder–Rojas–Yampolsky's prime-end
material was also checked. No injection on fundamental groups or descent
of a disk homotopy through this dense image is inferred.

## C.74: an exact obstruction to one principal interpolation

The original disk compactification is the explicit spiral coordinate
p(z)=z/(1+|z|) exp(i log(1+|z|)), with its given inverse. Three
out-and-back paths in the plane are horizontal translation, vertical
translation and a compact triangular horizontal shear. Their bounded
displacements give actual boundary-fixed closed-disk maps. They generate

Two actual principal sections of that same action are related by
(s,t) -> (s-t^3,t). For the literal linear interpolation in the changed
section, the original-coordinate midpoint is
(w,t) -> (w+3t/4+3/8,t+1/2). Its exact inverse is retained.
At each original boundary point, two explicitly named exponential
sequences have the same limit and distinct midpoint-image limits. The
phase discrepancy is log(5/4)-atan(3/4), with a strictly nonzero rational
bound proved by integration. Thus this particular map fails to extend
at every original boundary point.

Any compact family of original boundary-fixed maps, and every fixed
finite word family in it, converges uniformly to the same boundary point
on those sequences. Its filled hull is contained in its convex hull.
Consequently no such filled compact-word bound contains the specified
midpoint images. This proves the precise failure of that candidate
interpolation, while retaining the valid contraction of the original
loop. The producer passes 41 exact symbolic and rational checks.

## C.75: complete principal orbit strips and endpoint characters

For the actual one-dimensional closed free proper operator action, the

At a finite endpoint a, c_a(g)=u_g(a) is a continuous character. Its
image on a chosen base stabilizer is zero or all R. There are precisely

actual source word. The injectivity of the translation coordinate is

The original disconnected component operator, including negative lambda,

The D.71 scalar character is already integral in Z_q. The original

## D.74: full pants section centralizers

This proof removes the earlier full fixed-group gap. For each of the
three D.34 section representatives s_i, it proves C_F(s_i)=1 and
N_E(<s_i>)=<s_i> in the full profinite extension.

The relative coefficient module is the augmentation kernel of the
actual compact permutation module on the three coset spaces E/D_j.
Its restriction to F is identified with the three original peripheral
coset modules by explicit equivariant homeomorphisms. The action of E
on those coordinates retains the peripheral conjugators. Continuous
Hom from this kernel gives the discrete coefficient module for the
ordinary LHS sequence; the associated exact sequence gives the literal
boundary restriction maps.

Full completed Poincare–Lefschetz duality for the pants pair identifies
the two rows of that sequence with the duals of the ordinary F rows.
For the quotient C2, the positive cyclic cohomology pairing is the
perfect pairing on ker(1+s)/im(1+s). The absolute-relative cup product
and its derivation identity make the two potentially nonzero d2 maps
transposes. Their equal ranks, together with actual periodicity, give
equal stable absolute and relative dimensions for every finite self-dual
coefficient module. Localizing the actual long exact sequence shows
that boundary restriction has rank exactly half the boundary dimension.

Full Brown restriction identifies the high-degree cohomology of E with
that of the three full section normalizers. D.34's triangle gives two
boundary occurrences of each class. The six named conjugators and their
coefficient transports make the restriction map the direct sum of three
duplicated maps from normalizer cohomology to section cohomology. The
half-rank equality forces every such map to be onto.

For a proposed nonidentity commuter c, take a finite quotient E/N
separating c and retaining E/F=C2. The self-dual coset module on
(E/N)/<s_i> has two distinct section-fixed basis cosets, one moved to
the other by c. Their positive cyclic cohomology classes are distinct.
On the other hand, the literal inner-cochain action proves that every
normalizer restriction image is c-invariant. This contradicts the
surjectivity just proved. The witness detects every full element,
including elements invisible in ordinary mod-two abelianization.

Haran's original Proposition 2.2 now applies: the actual index-two
subgroup F is projective and all involutions are self-centralizing.
Thus E is real projective, with the finite embedding-problem maps and
solution convention stated explicitly. The quotient of any two solutions
to f=h^{-1}alpha(h) is alpha-fixed, proving that the D.34 coboundary h
is unique. D.44's first-symmetry solution is that same element. The
remaining equation is exactly
x^mu h y^mu h^{-1}=u(xy)^mu u^{-1}, retaining every full unit mu and
the original third cusp. Its nonemptiness and a free-product recognition

## D.75: the full negative torus cusp centralizer

The same coefficient calculation applies to the actual one-cusp
augmentation module of a negative once-punctured-torus involution. Its
restriction is the completed oriented genus-one pair with the original
commutator boundary. The two internal dihedral reflection classes fuse
to the same global cusp class by the actual D.58 element g. The identity
and g give the two specified cochain transports. In the two-section-class
case the other global normalizer contributes a separate Brown factor
with zero boundary component. The half-rank calculation therefore forces
the cusp normalizer-to-section map onto for every finite self-dual
coefficient module. The same separating coset module proves C_F(v)=1
for every cusp reflection v.

The fusion element g with gvg^{-1}=cv is consequently unique. For every
full unit mu, the two actual transporter sets are singletons:
a_(mu,0)=c^((mu-1)/2)g and
a_(mu,1)=c^((mu+1)/2)g^{-1}. The proof checks both exponents at every
prime and retains the literal labels v and cv. The primitive full
homology line of D.59 remains unchanged.

The entire negative peripheral-conjugator existence problem is now the
conjugacy c^(-mu)a_(mu,i) conjugate to a_(mu,i), for some full unit and
one of the two labels. This is exactly the existence of an outside
centralizer element. D.62 supplies its full procyclic kernel. In the
two-class case the D.60 character gives the unique commuting outside
involution; in the one-class case the exact primewise correction gives
the unique full square root. Both D.61 model maps, their commutator
images c^mu and their inverse extension maps are retained. The one-class
extension is real projective by Haran's criterion. The other fixed group
in the two-class case retains its cohomology and homology surjection;
the proof establishes neither its procyclic recognition nor existence
of the remaining conjugacy. No numerical verification is claimed.

## F.19: the exact joint kernel and two split source comparisons

The original ten-point forgetting map surjects onto the actual fiber
product of the eight- and six-point completions over their common

configuration space, its section and both split-completion maps using

Their eight global winding characters send them to the standard basis.

The retained six-point curve is the full pure centralizer of its labelled

fixed subgroup. An actual D+ cocycle has a unique corrector in the
larger kernel; its image in the smaller kernel is fixed and therefore
trivial. The corrector belongs to D+. This proves the unique continuous
corrector map and the isomorphism from the full centralizer onto the
fixed fiber product.

Consequently the F.18 correction lies in D+, and its exact source
obstruction has target D+/K+. Keeping the eleven old source meridians
gives an explicit section of the rank-27 to rank-11 filling map.
Its image supplies common splittings of the drilled source image and
the full two-track pullback, with kernels K+ and D+ respectively.
Both inverse semidirect-product maps are displayed. The pullback's
centralizer is exactly the canonical branch lift of the original
residual equalizer. The producer passes 44 exact rational and winding
checks. Ambient normal generation of D+ supplies no unstated equality
with K+; the original source-membership and residual-triviality

## C.76: controlled base lifting with the original source topology

The source is the group of homeomorphisms of the actual closed disk fixing its boundary pointwise and commuting on the interior with a specified closed, locally compact, free proper operator group of dimension one. The principal coordinates, their inverse and every component operator are those already constructed in C.75. For a based loop, the fixed base set A and the complementary base intervals are retained. The first relative filling includes the original boundary, the lines over A, and the intervals for which the endpoint characters either vanish or are forced by the moving base.

The new proof uses actual endpoint translation characters before taking closure. Their image is a real vector space of dimension at most two, by the existing finite-word additive path argument. Short differences of original loop values span that same space. Six short differences supply a neighborhood of zero, so arbitrarily small changes in a desired character value have arbitrarily small original disk representatives. Successive corrections are chosen summably small in a complete metric of the actual group. Their finite products and the initial bounded-word representatives lie in one compact, path-connected source family K. This construction retains convergence of inverses as part of its topology.

At a fixed target base point, allowed fiber displacements form convex intervals obtained from labels in K satisfying the exact base or character condition. A small actual group correction works for all labels at once; it proves the interlaced lower-limit inclusion used in the relative selection argument. The selected fiber displacement therefore has the exact identity and original-loop boundary values. The displayed skew map and its displayed inverse are continuous jointly in every parameter.

Every component operator is retained through its actual formula (s,t)↦(λs+κ,t+d), including negative λ. On a full base line preserved by such an operator, the selection is made in the actual line bundle with its positive norm. Compact three-word bounds in the original disk group control every approach to the original circle. Finite endpoint limits use the exact character equations and uniform continuity of the compact source family. The theorem is stated through the restriction map to the specified closed invariant subset; no extension over an omitted strip is inferred.

## C.77: abelian base actions and exact scalar comparison spaces

C.77 includes every interval with an abelian effective base action. Transitivity and freeness make evaluation an actual topological group isomorphism with the real translation group. The character d_I of that action is retained together with the finite endpoint characters, giving a vector of dimension at most three. The five-letter additive path formula and fifteen short increments provide the common compact-family construction for this enlarged vector.

The remaining nonabelian base case has a useful exact action. The derived subgroup of the actual loop-generated group is path connected. Its nontrivial normal base image has no common fixed point, and its interval orbits are open, hence it acts transitively on the whole base interval. Endpoint characters vanish on this subgroup. Consequently the scalar character/base action on the specified plane is transitive, with its original kernel and stabilizer. The endpoint evaluation map from the Polish based-path group is open. The already proved compact-preimage lemma supplies compact lifts of compact targets inside prescribed path neighborhoods; no local continuous section is asserted.

These comparisons provide the exact source maps used in the next two claims. They also show explicitly how the new restriction target contains the preceding one. Independently selected contractions are not identified with one another merely because both have the same endpoint values.

## C.78: one endpoint-character direction and compact pair transport

C.78 includes every interval whose actual endpoint-character image has dimension at most one, together with all abelian base intervals. The principal new base construction embeds the common compact source family K in Hilbert space using a countable dense collection of continuous scalar functions. For a compact character fiber F, the closed convex hull of its image is compact: finite nets give finite-dimensional compact approximations with uniformly small errors. A point of this convex hull defines a unique positive normalized average of every continuous function on K. Uniform approximation establishes both uniqueness and joint continuity.

The average of the base evaluations is strictly increasing. For two fixed input points, every label has a strictly positive base gap, and compactness gives a positive minimum. Compact inverse envelopes give the correct interval endpoints and joint inverse continuity. The base map therefore remains an actual increasing homeomorphism, with all relative parameter values fixed exactly.

The proof then establishes a compact local pair-transport lemma for countably many comparison spaces at once. The source is the actual Polish based-path group. At each stage, compact target sets have compact lifts inside a prescribed small path neighborhood. A countable compact exhaustion and neighborhoods shrinking to the identity produce one compact family of path values. Its product with its inverse moves any sufficiently close pair near each fixed comparison point, with the moving element arbitrarily close to the identity in the original group. The proof uses a finite cover and a common refinement at each finite stage; it does not require a continuously chosen transporter.

A second summably convergent compact product supplies a common family M of original maps realizing all corrected targets. Its displacement fibers satisfy the required lower-limit inclusion. Relative convex selection then gives the actual fiber coordinate, and the original source-plane triangle argument bounds the selected maps by MM^-1M and their inverses by M^-1MM^-1. Proportional endpoint characters give both finite endpoint limits. The original circle limits follow from compactness of those actual word families, every member of which fixes the circle pointwise.

## C.79: both independent endpoint characters and the complete disk loop filling

The only intervals remaining after C.78 have nonabelian base image and two independent finite endpoint characters. Such an interval is bounded. C.79 first selects a positive average in the full two-character fiber. This produces one common increasing base map B. It then performs two scalar character/base selections, one with the left character and one with the right character, using that same B and that same compact family M.

The cutoff θ(t)=max(0,min(1,3(t−a)/(b−a)−1)) gives the fiber displacement U=(1−θ)U_a+θU_b. Both scalar selections lie in convex intervals of original source displacements at exactly the same base level. Their blend stays in the convex interval at that level. C.62 therefore supplies the same compact three-word bound for the blended map and the same inverse bound; coordinate interpolation is not used without that actual source comparison.

Two sequence arguments give the finite endpoint limits. Within one interval, the cutoff equals the appropriate endpoint choice in a whole neighborhood of that endpoint. Along pairwise distinct bounded intervals approaching a fixed base line, both endpoints tend to that line. Otherwise a fixed point on one side of the line would belong to infinitely many disjoint intervals. Compactness and the exact character values make both scalar selections tend to the same required displacement, regardless of the intervening cutoff values. The compact inverse base envelopes give the corresponding inverse limits.

The cutoff and both scalar constraints respect the actual component transport, including negative fiber multiplier. The maps paste with the lines over A and the original circle. The result is a based contraction of every based loop in the specified actual disk centralizer. Thus its fundamental group at the identity is zero. The proof has passed the root audit and the independent audit in work/part_a/review_c79.md.

## C.80: every positive based homotopy group of that disk centralizer

C.80 carries the complete construction out for a based continuous map from S^n, for every n≥1. The new short increment set uses pairs of sphere parameters at distance at most δ<π. The pair space contracts to its diagonal by the unique shortest geodesic, and is compact and path connected. Subdivision of a sphere path provides the exact telescoping character identity. The same five-letter and fifteen-increment bounds follow in the original group topology.

The complete relative subset of [0,1]×S^n is ({0,1}×S^n)∪([0,1]×{*}). Its singleton labels agree on intersections and are continuous. The relative selection proof is checked on this closed subset itself. It uses paracompactness and summable approximation, so it does not depend on having a rectangular loop-parameter boundary.

On a nonabelian interval with zero endpoint-character vector, the base map is (1−r)t+rβ_ξ(t). It is strictly increasing, proper in the interval and has a jointly continuous inverse. A path in the sphere from its basepoint to ξ supplies an original family label at every intermediate base value. Only pointwise existence of that label is used; no continuously chosen sphere paths enter the construction. All other base prescriptions, character fibers, compact products, endpoint splices and component equations retain their already proved forms.

The same compact original word families control all boundary limits, jointly in the sphere parameters. The cone on S^n gives a disk filling, and the basepoint ray remains the identity. The exact conclusion is π_n(C_Q,1)=0 for n≥1 for the stated actual closed-disk group. Neither a description of π_0 nor a single contraction of the whole group is asserted. ## D.76: the full section augmentation module and its specified incidence map

The input is the actual full pants extension E=Fhat_2⋊C2 and its three specified section subgroups T_i. D.74 gives their complete self-normalizers and the finite-coefficient Brown comparison. Let k=F2, R=k[[E]], and Δ be the augmentation kernel of the sum of the three compact permutation modules k[[E/T_i]]. D.76 proves a continuous R-module isomorphism R^2→Δ.

The proof first keeps the exact restriction to Fhat_2. Each coset module becomes the regular compact F module. The displayed coordinate map identifies the augmentation kernel with I_F⊕R_F^2. The universal continuous crossed-homomorphism property identifies I_F with R_F^2 on the actual generators x−1 and y−1. Thus the restricted module is free of rank four.

For every finite E-module M, the explicit coefficient sequence 0→M→M⊗k[E/F]→M→0 has connecting cochain equal to cup product by the actual quotient character. Shapiro makes its middle degree-two cohomology vanish. Full finite-coefficient Brown restriction and period-one cohomology of the three sections make degree-one restriction onto their cohomologies surjective. The augmentation sequence consequently gives Ext^1_R(Δ,M)=0. The proof spells out the compact linear splitting and continuous translate cocycle that establish this bridge.

Compatible finite lifts then split a compact free presentation of Δ. The compatibility argument uses nonempty finite sets and common finer quotients; it does not assume that their transition maps are surjective. Projectivity and Frobenius reciprocity give dim Hom_R(Δ,M)=2 dim M for every finite module M. At each finite group-ring quotient, the projective coinvariant module has the simple top of a rank-two free module. Nakayama and projectivity give an actual finite isomorphism. A second compatible finite-family argument gives the full inverse-limit isomorphism and its continuous inverse.

The specified incidence map d_T sends the two basis vectors to e_1−e_0 and e_2−e_0. If H is the closed subgroup generated by the three specified section representatives, its cokernel is exactly the augmentation kernel of k[[E/H]]. Both comparison maps are written out: the common coset class is fixed by each T_i and hence by H. Consequently d_T is invertible exactly when H=E. The arbitrary free-module isomorphism does not substitute for this specified incidence map.

Four actual involutions s,xs,fs,yfs generate E, and the unique coboundary h gives fs=h^-1sh, exhibiting the duplicated class. The displayed triple s,xs,hyh^-1s generates K⋊C2 with K=closure<x,hyh^-1>. The proof retains the unresolved equality K=F and the original third-cusp equation. D.76 has passed the root audit and the independent full audit in work/part_b/review_d76.txt. It uses the previously checked full Brown and relative-module sources, and requires no new numerical certificate or bibliography entry.

### C.83: a continuous infinite evaluation loop with no original-disk lift

The example specifies the actual compactification, operator group, transitive commuting group, vertex labels and both topologies. Its plane coordinate is the homeomorphism

\[
j(z)=\frac{z}{1+\lVert z\rVert},\qquad
j^{-1}(w)=\frac{w}{1-\lVert w\rVert}
\]

from the plane to the interior of the original closed unit disk. The operator group is the conjugate through this map of integer horizontal translations. It is closed, discrete, free and proper on the interior. All plane translations extend through this same compactification by the identity on the original circle, and provide the actual transitive commuting subgroup required by C.81. The quantitative extension estimate is

\[
\lVert j(z+v)-j(z)\rVert\le
\frac{2\lVert v\rVert}{1+\lVert z\rVert}.
\]

It controls the maps and their inverses, jointly over compact translation-parameter sets.

The invariant grid has labelled vertices \((k/4,l)\), indexed by a fixed enumeration of \(\mathbb Z^2\). The generator of the operator group acts by the literal label change \((k,l)\mapsto(k+4,l)\). Dividing each rectangle by the same diagonal gives a quotient triangulation. Each closed lifted vertex star has horizontal width \(1/2<1\), so it projects injectively under the unit deck translation. The stars have a common diameter bound in the plane. The displayed radial estimate makes their original-disk diameters tend to zero as the vertices escape, proving the finite-large-star property of C.82 for this exact carrier.

The target is the actual simultaneous evaluation image, with the subspace topology of ordered-coordinate convergence and vague convergence of its counting measures. The evaluation map from the original disk group is continuous: coordinate convergence follows from uniform convergence of maps; for any compactly supported test function, uniform convergence of inverse maps confines all relevant original vertices to one compact interior neighborhood. Thus the test-function sum eventually involves a fixed finite set of labels. This proves continuity of the actual map in both components of the specified target topology.

Put \(b(u)=\max(0,1-4|u|)\). The explicit shear family

\[
F_{n,s}(x,y)=(x+sn\,b(y-n),y),\qquad 0\le s\le1
\]

has inverse with the minus sign and commutes with every integer horizontal translation. For each fixed \(n\), its displacement is bounded by \(n\); the radial estimate therefore gives an actual continuous family of boundary-fixed disk homeomorphisms, including its inverses. Only the grid row \(l=n\) moves.

On the interval \([2^{-n-1},2^{-n}]\), the amplitude rises linearly from zero to one and returns to zero. Concatenate these evaluated paths as the intervals accumulate at zero. Every fixed finite set of labels is eventually fixed. Every compact planar test support eventually misses the moving row and all its images, since the shear preserves the second coordinate. These two exact facts prove continuity of the concatenated loop in the stated evaluation-image topology. Multiplying all amplitudes by \(1-r\), for \(0\le r\le1\), gives an explicit based null-homotopy in that same image; the continuity estimates are uniform in \(r\).

At the peak time \(t_n=3\,2^{-n-2}\), every putative lift must send the vertex \(j(0,n)\) to \(j(n,n)\). Its uniform original-disk displacement is consequently at least

\[
\lVert j(n,n)-j(0,n)\rVert
\ge\frac{n}{1+\sqrt2\,n}>\frac13.
\]

Since \(t_n\to0\), no lift starting at the identity is continuous. The bound applies to every homeomorphism with the required labelled evaluation, regardless of its behavior between vertices. It proves failure of path lifting and hence of the Serre and Hurewicz fibration properties for this exact evaluation map. The target loop is explicitly null-homotopic. No nonzero homotopy class in the original centralizer is claimed.

The certificate `code/part_b/c83_evaluation_path_certificate.py` passes 143 exact symbolic and rational checks. Root and the independent reviewer both read the proof and producer and reran it. The checks verify the radial inverse, shear identities, literal labels, time intervals, row values and displacement formulas. The written proof establishes the infinite continuity and universal nonlifting statements. The original Teh definition on printed pages 5–6 of arXiv:2602.07362v1 was reopened; only its topology definitions are used in C.83.

### F.21: transitivity on the full divisor cosets and normal generation in the joint kernel

F.20 had proved transitivity on its specified finite quotient families. F.21 proves the actual source action transitive on each entire discrete cross-divisor coset space, and then on the corresponding full profinite coset space. It retains all four old labels, both new-point signs and the original chosen source meridians and whiskers.

For each old label \(u\in\mu_5\setminus\{\zeta\}\), the coordinates are

\[
A(s)=\frac{\zeta-s}{\zeta+s},\quad
C_u(s)=\frac{u-s}{u+s},\quad
B=A^2,\quad U=C_u^2,\quad V=\epsilon A.
\]

The argument is based first at the actual selected collision point \(d\) in the filled source \(Y\). At that point \(U=V\), giving a literal common diagonal basepoint. The original source whisker and its image in the configuration space transport every group and forgetting map back to the original basepoint. The selected meridian is retained through these specified transports.

The fourfold square-root cover of \(\mathcal M_{0,5}\) is

\[
W=\{(A,C):A,C\notin\{0,\infty,1,-1\},\ C\ne\pm A\}.
\]

It maps through the intermediate double cover \(W_A\) by \((A,C)\mapsto(A,C^2)\). The actual pencil

\[
\kappa(A,C)=\frac{C-A}{1-AC}
\]

takes values in \(\mathscr B=\mathbb P^1\setminus\{0,1,-1\}\). Removing \(E=\{AC=1\}\) gives a locally trivial bundle over \(\mathscr B\setminus\{\infty\}\). Its fiber is the sphere with the eight specified punctures \(0,\infty,1,-1,-k,-1/k\) and the two roots of \(kA^2+2A+k=0\). Their distinctness, local root choices and disjoint puncture isotopies provide explicit bundle charts.

The old source lies on the fiber \(k_u=(u-\zeta)/(u+\zeta)\). In its original \(s\)-coordinate that entire fiber removes exactly \(0,\infty,\pm\zeta,\pm u,\pm t_u\), where \(t_u^2=\zeta u\). The map from \(Y\) fills the remaining four punctures, so its fundamental-group map onto the fiber image is onto. The divisor \(E\) is connected and smooth, and the derivative of \(1/\kappa\) transverse to it is nonzero. Filling it kills exactly the normal closure of its meridian, whose base image is one meridian of infinity. The resulting actual quotient maps prove

\[
N=\operatorname{im}\bigl(\pi_1(Y,d)\to J=\pi_1(W)\bigr)
 =\ker\bigl(\kappa_*:J\to\pi_1(\mathscr B,k_u)\bigr).
\]

This is a discrete equality; the subsequent proof does not require exactness after completing this pencil-kernel sequence.

The new section of \(W_A\) is \(A\mapsto(A,\epsilon A)\). Its pullback to \(W\) is the connected double cover \(r\mapsto\epsilon r^2\) on the sphere minus \(0,\infty,\pm1,\pm i\), with the literal map \(r\mapsto(\epsilon r^2,r)\). The pencil on this pullback is

\[
f_\epsilon(r)=\frac{r}{r^2+\epsilon r+1}.
\]

Deleting the extra target branch value \(\epsilon/3\) makes it an unramified degree-two cover. The meridian of that value has odd covering monodromy and is killed by the actual target filling. Multiplying a base element by that meridian to the negative of its parity proves that the cover subgroup maps onto the filled-base fundamental group.

Writing \(T=\pi_1(W_A)\) and \(M\) for the new-section subgroup, the precise factorizations are

\[
J=N(M\cap J),\qquad T=NM.
\]

The diagonal right-coset coordinate is \((b_o,b_n)\operatorname{diag}\mapsto b_nb_o^{-1}\), with inverse \(v\mapsto(1,v)\operatorname{diag}\). In this coordinate the actual source acts by \(v\mapsto m(a(n))vn^{-1}\). Factor \(v^{-1}=nm\) in \(T=NM\) and project to the \(A\)-coordinate. This forces \(m=m(a(n))^{-1}\), so \(v\) is the image of the identity under the source action. It proves discrete transitivity. The full completion comparison already proved in F.19–F.20 identifies the full divisor coset with \(\widehat F_3\). The compact source orbit contains the dense discrete orbit and is therefore the whole coset space.

A small oriented normal circle bundle over the regular part of each cross divisor gives actual lifts of its loops that centralize the selected meridian. Its base projection is onto on fundamental groups, its fiber generator is central, and the simple holomorphic source collision identifies that generator with the original positive primitive meridian. Completing this homomorphism gives a compact centralizing image whose projection contains the full divisor subgroup; injectivity of the circle-bundle completion is not required.

Let \(N_D\) be the closed normal closure of \(K^+\) inside \(D^+\). Source transitivity and the centralizer lift factor every ambient element as \(g=dhc\), where \(d\in D^+\), \(h\) lies in the actual drilled source image and \(c\) centralizes the chosen meridian \(\nu\). Hence

\[
g\nu g^{-1}=d(h\nu h^{-1})d^{-1}\in N_D.
\]

The middle conjugate lies in \(K^+\) by its normality in that source image. F.19's eight ambient normal generators therefore all have their conjugates in \(N_D\), proving

\[
\overline{\langle\!\langle K^+\rangle\!\rangle}_{D^+}=D^+.
\]

Passing to the abelianization proves that the actual image of \(K^+\) is the entire \(D^+_{\mathrm{ab}}\). The image is also full in every finite nilpotent quotient: a proper image would lie in a maximal subgroup, and the explicit upper-central-series argument proves that every such maximal subgroup is normal, contradicting normal generation. Compactness then gives the actual surjection onto the maximal pronilpotent quotient of \(D^+\).

The symbolic producer `code/part_a/cross_divisor_geometry_f21.py` passes 95 exact rational and cyclotomic checks, independently rerun by root and the peer reviewer. It checks all four source fibers, the puncture indexing, both collision factorizations, the pencil inverse and reduced divisor derivative, and both degree-two section maps. The bundle, group quotient, orbit, full completion and centralizer arguments are proved in the text. Equality \(K^+=D^+\) remains unresolved.

### Source audit of the two infinite-configuration preprints

Both exact versioned PDFs were opened: arXiv:2512.21498v1 and arXiv:2602.07362v1. The attached source-audit notes give explicit counterexamples to specified statements, together with a rational certificate passing 202 checks. None of these refutations is used to infer failure of configuration-space asphericity.

For the collar assertions, take \(F(t)=\{1-t\}\cup\{10,11,\ldots\}\) on \([0,1/8]\). Every compactly supported test sum is a single continuous term plus a fixed finite sum. The source's one-sided collar supremum is \(3/4\) at zero and \(1-t\) for positive \(t\). This directly contradicts its asserted upper semicontinuity; a continuous strict separator below one is also impossible. The data satisfy the exact hypotheses of the 2025 Lemma 5.10 and the 2026 Lemma 6.7.

For the 2026 Lemma 6.8, move only strand four along the supplied rational lasso around strand one, with winding zero around strand two. Its first three strands stay fixed, so the class lies in the required finite-truncation kernel. The asserted representative would keep the fourth strand outside the radius-two disk while fixing the first two at one and two. Interpolating between these two fixed points gives a nonzero homotopy of pair differences and forces their winding numbers equal, contradicting the retained values one and zero.

An infinite sequence of these finite lassos also refutes the precise Cauchy-realization assertion in Theorem 6.14 and the completeness assertion for the displayed metric in Theorem 6.18. The required compatible finite data demand unequal windings around the first two strands for every later strand. For any actual continuous configuration loop, coordinate and vague continuity force uniform escape of all sufficiently late labels from each compact set. Those late strands then avoid a disk containing both first-strand tracks; their pair windings must agree by the same interpolation. Thus the compatible finite data have no actual realization. This does not decide the existence of another complete metric for the quotient topology.

The complete notes retain each theorem number, printed page, version, exact formula and failed step. C.83 uses only the 2026 topology definitions. No asphericity, infinite normalization, inverse-limit realization or completeness theorem from these preprints is imported.

### Remaining obligations and current validation

C.12 still requires the general admissible-surface level-zero comparison and the controlled deformation of arbitrary original families into the lifted-skeleton stabilizer, together with the stated frontier comparisons. ## Exact source counterexamples for the infinite-configuration route

PRIMARY-SOURCE AUDIT: THE TWO TEH INFINITE-CONFIGURATION PREPRINTS
Status: proved counterexamples to specified normalization and realization
assertions; no conclusion that configuration-space asphericity fails.
This is an unnumbered source-check note. C83 is a separate, self-contained
actual evaluation-map obstruction and imports no theorem from either text.

PRIMARY FILES AND PRECISE LOCATORS

[T25] Jyh-Haur Teh, The asphericity of locally finite infinite configuration
spaces and Weierstrass entire coverings, arXiv:2512.21498v1, 25 December 2025:
https://arxiv.org/pdf/2512.21498v1
https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.21498
The original PDF has 49 pages. Definition 3.1 and Definition 3.4 are on
printed page 7 (PDF extraction lines 473-477 and 510-515). Lemma 5.10 and
its proof are on printed pages 25-26 (lines 1784-1880). Its use in Lemma
5.12 is on printed page 27 (lines 1918-1933).

[T26] Jyh-Haur Teh, Topological fundamental groups of locally finite
infinite configuration spaces and infinite braids, arXiv:2602.07362v1,
7 February 2026:
https://arxiv.org/pdf/2602.07362v1
https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.07362
The original PDF has 43 pages. Definition 2.6 is on printed page 5,
and the ordered sum metric equation (2) is on printed page 6
(lines 309-347). Lemma 6.7 is on printed pages 26-27
(lines 2168-2283); Lemma 6.8 is on printed pages 28-29
(lines 2285-2324). Theorem 6.14 is on printed pages 31-32
(lines 2477-2535 onward). Definition 6.17 and Theorem 6.18
are on printed pages 32-33 (lines 2557-2605).

All line references above are to the web tool's extraction of these exact
versioned PDFs, not to a later revision. Printed pages are one greater than
the zero-based PDF page indices. The observed records identify preprints;
no refereed-publication status is assumed.

1. EXACT CONFIGURATION SPACE AND TOPOLOGY

Fix a literal enumeration of labels by positive integers. A configuration
z=(z_i) is an infinite sequence of distinct complex numbers, with only
finitely many z_i in each compact planar set. Its counting measure is
mu_z=sum_i delta_(z_i). Write

 d_prod(z,w) = sum_(i>=1) 2^(-i) min(1, |z_i-w_i|).

For a countable compactly supported test family (phi_j) detecting vague
convergence, put

 d_V(mu,nu) = sum_(j>=1) 2^(-j)
              |integral phi_j d(mu-nu)| /
              (1+|integral phi_j d(mu-nu)|),
 d_Sigma(z,w) = d_prod(z,w)+d_V(mu_z,mu_w).

Thus the topology simultaneously requires coordinate convergence and
vague counting-measure convergence. These are the definitions at the
locators above. The point zero is allowed. The positive-integer sequence
(1,2,3,...) is a chosen base configuration for the fundamental groups,
not a restriction on every point of the configuration space. Neither
collar lemma requires that base configuration. The explicit collar
example below is even nonzero and has a fixed positive-integer tail.

2. COLLAR ASSERTIONS: COMPLETE DATA USED IN THE AUDIT

T25 Lemma 5.10:
R>0; epsilon>0; R/2+epsilon<R and 2R<3R-epsilon;
A={R<=|z|<=2R}, B={R/2<=|z|<=3R};
K compact metric; F:K->unordered configurations vague-continuous.
Its functions are

 u_in(x)=sup{|z|: z in F(x), R/2-epsilon<=|z|<R/2},
          with empty value R/2-epsilon;
 v_out(x)=inf{|z|: z in F(x), 3R<|z|<=3R+epsilon},
          with empty value 3R+epsilon.

The assertions are USC(u_in), LSC(v_out), and continuous separators
u_in<r_in<R/2 and 3R<r_out<v_out. The stated radial isotopy starts at
identity, fixes A and radii <=R/2-epsilon or >=3R+epsilon, is supported
in the two closed epsilon-collars, and sends radii r_in,r_out to R/2,3R
at time one, making the resulting configuration avoid boundary(B).

T26 Lemma 6.7:
K compact metric; F vague-continuous; R,epsilon>0;
u(x)=sup{|z|:z in F(x), R-epsilon<=|z|<R}, empty value R-epsilon.
It asserts USC(u). For closed L with F(L) avoiding the closed
epsilon-collar, it asserts continuous r with u<r<R on K\L and r=R
on L, and a radial isotopy supported in that collar, stationary on L,
starting at identity, whose time-one inverse takes radius R to r(x).

COUNTEREXAMPLE AND DIRECT SUPREMUM CALCULATION

Set K=[0,1/8] and

 F(t)={1-t} union {10,11,12,...}.

This is infinite, simple, locally finite, nonzero and labelled by
(1-t,10,11,...). Every compactly supported test function phi gives

 integral phi d(mu_(F(t))) = phi(1-t)+sum_(m>=10) phi(m),

where the displayed tail sum is fixed and finite. Hence F is
vague-continuous. Its only varying ordered coordinate is the first,
so it is also continuous in the exact ordered sum topology.

For T25 take R=2 and epsilon=1/4. The required inequalities are
1+1/4<2 and 4<6-1/4. The inner collar is [3/4,1) in radius. At t=0
it is empty; at every 0<t<=1/8 it contains exactly the point 1-t.
Consequently

 u_in(0)=3/4,
 u_in(t)=1-t  (0<t<=1/8),
 limsup_(t downarrow 0) u_in(t)=1>3/4.

For T26 take R=1, epsilon=1/4, and the allowed closed set L empty.
Its u is exactly the same function.

Moreover, any continuous r with u(t)<r(t)<1 would have r(0)>=1,
because the positive-t inequalities force liminf_(t downarrow 0)r(t)>=1.
This contradicts r(0)<1. Thus both semicontinuity and the required
continuous-separator assertion actually fail on these admissible data.

The printed proofs express {u<c} as a countably infinite intersection
of open compact-avoidance sets and then declare it open (T25 lines
1816-1828; T26 lines 2194-2206). Here c=7/8 gives
{t:u(t)<c}={0} on [0,1/8], which is not open. This computes the
specific failed openness step rather than merely questioning an
application of an insertion theorem.

3. TAIL-REPRESENTATIVE ASSERTION AND PAIR-WINDING COUNTEREXAMPLE

T26 Lemma 6.8 asserts:
for n>=1 and g in V_n=ker(p_n), a representative gamma exists with
gamma_k(t)=k for k<=n and gamma_m(t) outside K_(n-1) for m>n,
where K_r is the closed disk of radius r.

Use n=3. At the base configuration (1,2,3,4,5,...), fix all strands
except strand 4. For strand 4 use the following rational polygonal
lasso; pairs list (real part, imaginary part):

 tail:
 (4,0), (4,1), (5/4,1), (5/4,1/4);
 square:
 (5/4,1/4), (3/4,1/4), (3/4,-1/4), (5/4,-1/4), (5/4,1/4);
 reversed tail back to (4,0).

All intermediate points avoid the other positive integers. The square
is positively oriented and encloses 1 but not 2 or 3. The tail and
its reverse cancel in every winding calculation. Thus the continuous
ordered-pair maps

 (z_i)_i |-> z_4-z_1,       (z_i)_i |-> z_4-z_2

to C\{0} give windings 1 and 0. Only one coordinate moves in a fixed
compact set, so the lasso is continuous in d_Sigma. Its first three
coordinates are constant and its class g belongs to V_3.

Any representative demanded by Lemma 6.8 would have its first two
coordinates equal to 1,2 and its fourth coordinate z(t) outside
the closed radius-two disk. Then

 (s,t) |-> z(t)-(1+s),       0<=s,t<=1,

is a homotopy of loops in C\{0}, since |z(t)|>2 and 1+s lies in
[1,2]. The two windings would therefore be equal. They are invariant
under every based configuration homotopy through the two displayed
continuous maps, contradicting their values 1 and 0. This refutes
the actual representative assertion with its prescribed finite labels.

The rational polygon is independently checked in
code/teh_configuration_audit_certificate.py. No numerical winding or
floating-point approximation enters the proof.

4. AN INDEPENDENT INFINITE REALIZATION AND COMPLETENESS FAILURE

The target assertion is T26 Theorem 6.14:
g_(n+1) in g_n V_n for all n implies intersection_n(g_n V_n) nonempty.
Its displayed metric in Definition 6.17 records the largest matching
finite truncation by d(g,h)=2^(-(N(g,h)+1)) for distinct g,h;
Theorem 6.18 asserts completeness for that metric.

For every m>=3, let a_m be a based configuration loop moving only
strand m along the preceding type of lasso once around 1, zero
around 2, with all other positive-integer strands fixed. The
same tail construction with (m,0),(m,1) in place of (4,0),(4,1)
works. Set g_1=g_2=1 and g_n=a_3 a_4 ... a_n for n>=3.
Then g_(n+1)=g_n a_(n+1) and a_(n+1) lies in V_n. Every g_n is
an actual class represented by a finite concatenation of continuous
locally finite configuration loops.

We prove directly the needed restriction on any actual continuous
configuration loop z(t), 0<=t<=1:

 For each compact planar K, only finitely many labels meet K
 at any time of the loop.

Otherwise take labels m_i tending to infinity, parameters t_i
converging to t_*, and z_(m_i)(t_i) converging to a point p of K.
Local finiteness at t_* allows a nonnegative compactly supported
function equal to one near p whose support contains at most the
single limiting configuration point p. If p is absent at t_*,
its limiting integral is zero but the integrals along t_i are
at least one. If p is present with label j, coordinate continuity
and the extra labels m_i give integrals at least two while the
limiting integral is one. Each conclusion contradicts vague
continuity. This proves the restriction without importing T26.

Choose a closed disk containing both first-strand and second-strand
tracks of z, and enlarge it slightly. All sufficiently late
strands avoid this disk uniformly. For such an m, the formula

 (s,t) |-> z_m(t)-((1-s)z_1(t)+s z_2(t))

is nonzero throughout the square. It proves equality of the two
pair windings of strand m with strands 1 and 2.

A class in every g_n V_n would instead have winding 1 with strand 1
and winding 0 with strand 2 for every m>=3, by choosing n>=m.
This contradicts the restriction just proved. Thus the asserted
intersection is empty. In the displayed finite-truncation formula,
(g_n) is Cauchy because g_n^(-1)g_m lies in V_n for m>=n.
Convergence in that formula would force the impossible common
finite-truncation data. Consequently its claimed completeness fails.
This argument does not require injectivity of the map to the
finite-truncation inverse limit.

The same winding data also appear in T26 Construction 4.6 and
Proposition 4.9. The direct argument above establishes the conflict
without relying on either source proof. It does not assert that the
quotient topology on the topological fundamental group lacks some
other complete compatible metric.

5. FURTHER PROOF-CHECK BOUNDARY AND USABLE FINITE MAPS

The infinite straightening in T26 Proposition 5.9 was also inspected.
In its compact-support induction, for a fixed target disk the text
first controls the supports S_k up to L, obtains an escape index M_R,
and starts the induction at n_start=max{L,M_R,j+2}+1. The induction
then assumes S_k avoids the target disk for every L<k<n.
At n=n_start this already includes the intermediate earlier stages
L+1,...,n_start-1, whose avoidance has not been established by those
choices. This is an unproved induction input in the written argument,
not an explicit refutation of its injectivity conclusion. A single
globally consistent construction for all target disks would also
have to be retained.

The finite-truncation local constructions in T26 Section 3 use
compactly supported finite-point ambient maps and give explicit
finite-configuration bundle charts. The local formulas were read;
they do not themselves display a lift continuous in the original
closed-disk map-and-inverse topology of C81.

C83 addresses that exact next map. Its actual evaluation source,
operator labels, target subspace topology and explicit nonliftable
path are all specified and proved independently. The source
counterexamples above are not used to infer C83.

SCOPE

No theorem of either preprint has been imported into an accepted
fragment. The explicit failed statements above prevent their
displayed proof chains from supplying the remaining C12 infinite
configuration argument. No conclusion here refutes asphericity
of the configuration spaces themselves, and none identifies the
general actual centralizer's positive homotopy groups.
