AI-produced by GPT-6 Astra (OpenAI, ChatGPT Pro mode). No priority claimed.

The four new numbered results are C.81, C.82, D.77 and F.20. Each has a
complete written proof, matching result metadata, root review and an
independent mathematical review. Their addition gives 248 numbered claims:
217 Proved, 3 Known, 25 Refuted and 3 Open. There are 75 bibliography
entries and 36 manuscript-reading rows. The report retains its initial
INCOMPLETE marker and the full 39-obligation register. The three open rows
remain C.12, D.12 and F.7, with the exact remaining maps described below.

## C.81: the actual discrete-operator metric and finite configurations

The source is the original group of homeomorphisms of a closed disk that
fix its circle pointwise and commute on the interior with a specified
closed discrete free proper operator group Q. The theorem explicitly
retains the additional hypothesis that an actual subgroup of this source
acts transitively on the interior. The closure of that subgroup is a
Polish transitive group. Its open evaluation map supplies the small
actual elements used in the proof.

For the original compact-disk metric d, the invariant metric is

\[
d_Q(x,y)=\sup_{q\in Q}d(qx,qy).
\]

The identity term proves d ≤ d_Q. For an interior sequence x_j → x,
open evaluation supplies actual g_j → 1 with g_jx=x_j. Commutation gives

\[
d_Q(x_j,x)\leq\sup_{z\in\overline D}d(g_jz,z)\longrightarrow0.
\]

This proves the reverse topology comparison without selecting a
continuous section. The infimum of d_Q over an operator orbit gives
the actual quotient metric. Properness makes distinct orbit distances
positive. The image-of-balls equality proves the quotient topology;
the displayed r/5 estimate gives isometric covering sheets. Each fiber
is uniformly discrete, with its own separation constant.

Restriction of the original relative group to the interior satisfies
the exact equality

\[
\sup_xd_Q(hx,kx)=\sup_{z\in\overline D}d(hz,kz),
\]

and the same equality holds for the inverses. Thus the original group
has a closed isometric image in the uniform equivariant group. The
boundary condition of that image remains explicit. For trivial Q, a
nonidentity disk rotation gives an actual uniform interior map whose
circle values preclude membership in the specified image.

The compact-core estimate supplies the boundary control needed later.
For two compact interior cores choose compact sets S,T of actual
circle-fixed maps whose evaluations cover them. For z=sx_0 and z'=tx_0,

\[
qz'=ts^{-1}(qz).
\]

The same compact family TS^{-1} controls every operator q and every
pair of points in the two cores. Consequently every compact parameter
family supported above one fixed compact quotient set extends jointly,
with its inverses, by the identity on the original circle.

A based sphere family supported over one compact connected quotient
subsurface with nonempty boundary can therefore be filled upstairs.
The compact relative homeomorphism theorem supplies the downstairs
contraction. Multiplication by its value at the identity makes it based.
Covering homotopy lifting starts at the specified identity, preserves
all deck labels, and gives inverse homeomorphisms. The final deck
discrepancy is locally constant on the connected parameter sphere and
is one at its basepoint; hence the endpoint is the original family.
The compact-core estimate then proves continuity at the original circle.

Fixing finitely many operator orbits is handled through actual principal
evaluation bundles. Local supported bump translations in quotient disks
lift with the specified sheet labels. Their compact quotient supports
give continuity in the original disk topology. The displayed bundle
maps and their inverses prove local triviality. After the first orbit
is fixed, the relevant punctured plane is aspherical; its identity
homeomorphism component is contractible in the required cases. The
evaluation homomorphism on fundamental groups is zero through this
actual restriction map. The bundle exact sequence proves that fixing
any finite number of orbit labels preserves every positive based
homotopy group.

The proof also gives an explicit topology comparison needed for the
remaining work. On a noncompact quotient choose escaping, pairwise
disjoint disks with arbitrarily small d_Q lifts. Supported motions in
them lift to original maps h_j with both uniform distances from the
identity less than 1/j. A single positive continuous Whitney majorant
can exclude every descended motion. This establishes discontinuity of
the actual descent map into that topology, even on the stated subgroup
of lifts of compactly supported quotient isotopies.

The primary source checks retain their exact scope: Yagasaki's compact
relative lemma and noncompact-surface theorem; Definition 2.1 and the
compact-base hypothesis in his 2014 metric-cover theorem; and the
Whitney convention and fragmentation hypotheses in
Lech–Michalik–Rybicki 2018. C.81 proves its metric and compact-support
comparisons directly. It supplies no infinite-configuration lifting
theorem.

## C.82: null lifted triangles and the full skeleton stabilizer

The next result uses the same actual transitivity hypothesis. A proper
continuous exhaustion function on the quotient and its metric-cover
charts give an open cover with diameters tending to zero along the
quotient exhaustion. Radó's original surface triangulation lemma gives
a locally finite starting triangulation. Haver's subdivision lemma,
at k=0, makes every closed vertex star subordinate to that cover.

Lift the triangulation through the original covering. Every lifted
closed star lies in an injective covering sheet. Stars over vertices
escaping along the quotient exhaustion have d_Q-diameters tending to
zero. Stars over a bounded part of the quotient are translates of
finitely many compact cores. C.81's compact-family estimate makes these
translates shrink whenever their points approach the original circle.
Together these two estimates show that, for every positive epsilon,
only finitely many actual lifted closed stars have original d-diameter
at least epsilon. The same property holds for the closed triangles.

Let Lambda be the full lifted one-skeleton. Restrict an original
relative homeomorphism fixing Lambda pointwise to one chosen lift of
each quotient triangle. Conversely, paste any tuple of triangle
homeomorphisms relative to their boundaries using the prescribed deck
conjugations. A triangle has trivial deck stabilizer, so this formula
is unambiguous on its interior. The pieces agree on the fixed edges.
Local finiteness gives interior continuity; the nullity estimate gives
continuity at the original circle. Pasting the inverse tuple gives
the actual inverse homeomorphism.

These restriction and pasting maps identify the skeleton stabilizer,
with its original uniform map-and-inverse topology, with the product
of its triangle homeomorphism groups. The inverse topology is part of
the proof: outside finitely many large triangles any two pasted maps
and their inverses move a point within one uniformly small triangle.
On the finite remaining list, convergence of the corresponding factors
controls the actual fixed deck conjugations.

Coordinatewise Alexander deformation is jointly continuous in this
product topology. Its map and inverse displacements on the standard
disk are each bounded by twice the Alexander radius, independently of
the homeomorphism. Transporting this deformation through the proved
inverse maps contracts the entire actual skeleton stabilizer.

The remaining step is precise: a based family in the full original
relative centralizer must be moved into this stabilizer with coherent
vertex and edge motions and joint original-boundary control. C.81's
finite-label isomorphisms have not been passed to an inverse limit.
The contraction of the subgroup in C.82 does not provide that missing
deformation by itself.

## D.77: an actual full-quotient obstruction to a generation test

The standard extension is written as both the full coproduct of three
groups of order two and its actual rank-two free-kernel semidirect
product. The proof gives the maps and both inverse composites. Write
the involutions as a,b,c, put x=ba and y=ca, and let delta be conjugation
by a. Thus delta(x)=x^{-1} and delta(y)=y^{-1}.

Set g=x[x,y]x^{-1} and replace the third representative c by gcg^{-1}.
The two kernel generators of the resulting triple are x and

\[
Y=g\,y\,\delta(g)^{-1}.
\]

The three representatives still meet exactly the three original
involution classes. The closed subgroup they generate is the actual
semidirect product of the subgroup generated by x,Y with the section
generated by a. Its intersection with the original free kernel is
exactly that two-generator subgroup.

The endomorphism x↦x, y↦Y induces the identity on full abelianization.
For every prime p its maximal pro-p quotient map is onto: a proper
closed image would yield a nonzero mod-p character killing both images,
contradicting the displayed abelian coordinates. A finite-characteristic
quotient argument proves Hopficity and hence automorphy for every p.
This is an all-prime proof, independent of finite prime sampling.

The single actual quotient to A5 uses

\[
a=(12)(34),\quad b=(13)(25),\quad c=(14)(23),
\]

with product uv meaning u composed with v. Its exact permutation
calculation gives

\[
x=(15234),\quad y=(13)(24),\quad g=(132),\quad
gcg^{-1}=a,\quad Y=1.
\]

Both the original involutions and x,y generate all sixty elements of
A5. The altered triple generates the specified dihedral subgroup of
order ten in that same quotient, and its free-kernel subgroup has
cyclic image of order five. This proves both proper full inclusions.
The executed producer and both independent reruns pass all twenty
listed exact checks.

D.76's abstract rank-two free augmentation module still applies to
this example. Its specified incidence map, however, has cokernel
mapping onto the augmentation kernel of the six-coset permutation
module over F2. That target has dimension five. This identifies the
actual failure of that incidence map through its quotient map.

The original-cusp condition is retained: y has image of order two,
and every full unit power of y has that same image. Y has image one.
Thus Y is not conjugate in the full free group to any unit power of
the original y. The distinguished triple of D.76 has its own original
cusp alignment; this example proves the failure of the stated weaker
generation test and leaves equation D.74.40 unresolved.

## F.20: the exact source action and its continuous finite images

The chosen old/new cross divisor is v_1=a. Forgetting the other labels
gives the actual space M0,5 ×_{M0,4} M0,5, allowing equality of the two
selected moving points. Its diagonal inverse image has the proved
full induced topology. The completed right-diagonal coset coordinate
has the inverse maps

\[
(b_o,b_n)\operatorname{diag}(P_5)\longmapsto b_nb_o^{-1},
\qquad
v\longmapsto(1,v)\operatorname{diag}(P_5).
\]

It is the full free profinite group on the specified three meridians.
The erased conjugator of the fourth Artin meridian has inverse equal
to the actual point-pushing coordinate. The proof verifies that
coordinate on an explicit involutive free-basis substitution. The
source action is consequently

\[
v\longmapsto q_g^{-1}\alpha_g(v)p_g.
\]

Every old/new transport and the original-basepoint change is specified
as an actual path. The eleven source lassos form an embedded-star
geometric basis. The geometric producer works over the exact number
field Q(theta), with theta^4−5theta^2+5=0 and 19/10<theta<2. It certifies
112 forward segments, 672 strand-pair checks, 172 simple ordered
crossings and 72,380 exact field sign calls. The output retains the
complete projected and height polynomials, terminal crossing intervals
and their Bernstein signs, and counts of discarded root-free leaves;
the code reproduces all subdivisions. An earlier sentence overstated
retention of the discarded intervals and was corrected during audit.

The second exact producer adds 22 original-basepoint and one-point
transport sign checks. It verifies the complete eleven-generator
table, the actual strand deletions and the inverse point-pushing
substitution. It reads only the independent exact geometric output.
No exploratory numerical discovery file is an input to either producer.

The finite action is transitive on all 82 invariant A5 quotients among
the 1,668 epimorphism kernels of the rank-three free group, and on all
3,321 products of two distinct such quotients. The product test applies
each source generator to both coordinates simultaneously. Simplicity
and the distinct kernels prove that each product is an actual full
quotient.

Six further quotients are the mod-two homology covers over the invariant
A5 quotients retaining all four peripheral images. The actual Cayley
graph has sixty vertices and 180 oriented generator edges. Its cycle
space has dimension 121. The resulting finite group has order
60·2^121; the proof gives its chain-coordinate multiplication without
asserting that the extension splits. In every case the source filling
kernel acts by a translation subgroup projecting onto A5 with vector
kernel of dimension 119. The actual remaining source actions connect
the four right cosets. Hence all six complete actions are transitive.
There are 660 direct checks of the semilinear formulas against word
evaluation in these exact finite groups.

The bridge to the original subgroups K+ and D+ is also proved. Forgetting
the negative new point maps D+ onto the actual kernel of Fhat7→Fhat3.
The specified finite wreath assignment sends the selected old meridian
to a regular basis vector, the other old meridians to zero, and the
retained generators to their finite quotient values. It gives a
continuous homomorphism from D+ onto F_ell[Q] for every coefficient
prime ell. The image of K+ is exactly the span of the two actual
collision orbits under the displayed source action. Transitivity
therefore gives equal full K+ and D+ images for this specified family
of finite homomorphisms. The full right-coset membership problem in
D+/K+ remains unresolved.

## Full pants section module and its specified incidence map

D.76: exact full coefficient module after the pants fixed-subgroup theorem

The new proof identifies the actual section augmentation module
Delta_T with F2[[E]]^2. The argument uses only the specified pants
extension, D.74's full self-centralizers, and explicit coefficient
maps. It does not require an additional real-projective decomposition
theorem.

The short exact coefficient sequence
0 -> M -> M tensor F2[C2] -> M -> 0
has the actual quotient character u as its connecting cochain.
Its middle module is induction/coinduction from F, whose degree-two
cohomology vanishes. Hence cup-u is onto in degrees one to two.
Brown restriction in degree two and period-one cohomology of each
actual section imply degree-one restriction is onto. The actual
relative augmentation sequence gives Ext^1(Delta_T,M)=0 for every
finite M. Compatible finite lifts split a compact free presentation
of Delta_T, proving compact projectivity.

Restriction to the full free F gives a free module of rank four.
Frobenius reciprocity with the same coefficient sequence therefore
gives dim Hom_E(Delta_T,M)=2 dim M for all finite M. Every finite
group-ring coinvariant is projective; its semisimple top is the top
of a rank-two free module. Nakayama and projectivity give an actual
finite free isomorphism. Compactness of all finite isomorphism sets,
using common finer quotients rather than onto transition maps,
produces compatible isomorphisms and the claimed full inverse limit.

The proof separately keeps the actual incidence map formed from
e_1-e_0 and e_2-e_0. Its cokernel is precisely the augmentation kernel
of the coset space E/H for the closed subgroup H generated by those
three section representatives. It is onto, hence invertible after
the module identification, exactly when H=E. Thus the proof supplies
an exact module test for the remaining generation question, rather
than replacing that incidence map by an arbitrary free-module basis.

Four actual involutions s, xs, fs, yfs generate E. The unique D.74
element h gives fs=h^-1 s h, exhibiting the duplicated class.
The triple s, xs, h y h^-1 s generates K semidirect C2 with
K=closure<x,h y h^-1>. Neither K=F nor the remaining third-cusp
equation is asserted.

No new computational certificate or bibliography entry is required.
The original finite-coefficient Brown Theorem 2 and Section 1.1 were
reopened at https://mat.unb.br/~pz/arquivos/Claus3.pdf.
The accepted Wilkes version and its exact compact/discrete and
relative module conventions are already registered in D.74.

## Full pants section centralizers and real projectivity

D.74 — full pants section centralizers from twisted relative duality

Main proved result
For every nonidentity finite-order element of the full pure pants
loop-outer group, take the actual normalized involution and the three
literal D.34 section generators. Each centralizer in the full free
profinite kernel is trivial. The full section normalizers are exactly
their order-two subgroups.

Consequences retained with exact maps
Haran's Proposition 2.2 applies to the actual index-two free profinite
kernel and the proved self-centralizing involutions. Thus the exact
extension is real projective; D.74.38 spells out the given finite maps
pi:B->A and phi:E->A and the resulting gamma with pi gamma=phi.

D.34's coboundary h is unique even among all h in F. It lies in F',
and for GT0 it is the unique solution also satisfying D.44's first
symmetry. The remaining original peripheral equation is explicitly
D.74.40 with this h and the full unit exponent mu retained. Its
existence and a free-product recognition are not established here.

Proof mechanism
1. The exact E-set cosets E/D_j identify with F/L_j, including the
   transported E-action. Their augmentation kernel Delta is the actual
   relative module.
2. Hom_cont(Delta,M) gives the ordinary shifted relative cohomology.
   Its evaluation pairing is the actual absolute-relative cup pairing
   on the full completed pants pair.
3. Poincare–Lefschetz duality identifies the two relative fibre rows
   with the duals of the absolute fibre rows. Over F2 the relative
   orientation action is trivial.
4. In positive cyclic quotient columns, the norm-kernel/norm-image
   pairing with the dual is perfect. The LHS d2 derivation makes the
   two transgressions transpose. They need not vanish.
5. Their equal ranks give equal high absolute/relative dimensions.
   The actual u-periodic long exact sequence forces the rank of
   boundary restriction to be exactly half the boundary dimension.
6. Full finite-coefficient Brown restriction and the D.34 triangle
   identify this map with three restrictions, each duplicated twice
   under literal conjugator/coefficient transports. Each restriction
   to a finite section is therefore onto.
7. For a proposed nontrivial centralizer element c, a finite quotient
   J separates c and retains E/F. The self-dual module F2[J/S_i]
   has a fixed-coset cohomology class moved by c. Restriction from the
   normalizer is c-invariant, contradicting the proved surjectivity.

Source and audit record
The entire unnumbered proof snapshot is preserved as
work/part_c/pants_twisted_duality_note.tex.
Root and part_a_continue independently read the full proof and
reported PASS. The latter independently reopened all relevant
Wilkes clauses and the full finite-coefficient Brown theorem.

The June 19, 2018 accepted Wilkes version has a new distinct
bibliography key, C-WilkesRelativeAccepted2018, with published DOI
10.1016/j.jpaa.2018.07.001. Its proposition numbering is not silently
substituted into the older preprint citation. Haran's original author
PDF and Proposition 2.2 were also opened directly.

No finite computation is needed for this proof. Its finite coefficient
test is constructed for every separating quotient, and the periodic
cyclic cochain and bar-prism identities are written explicitly.


## Full negative torus cusp centralizer and exact transporters

D.75 — the full negative torus cusp centralizer

Proved
The actual cusp reflection v of every full negative once-punctured-torus
outer involution has C_F(v)=1. The same holds for every c^t v. The exact
fusion g v g^{-1}=c v has one and only one solution g in F.

The exact one-cusp comparison
Delta is the kernel of k[[E/D]]->k, with D=<c> semidirect <v>.
The map F/L -> E/D, fL -> fD, is an explicit homeomorphism with
transported action e(fL)=efv^{-u(e)}L. Thus D74's ordinary Hom(Delta,M)
coefficient spectral sequence and cup duality apply to the actual
completed genus-one one-boundary pair. The two transgressions are
transpose, giving equal high absolute/relative dimensions and an exact
half-dimension rank for boundary restriction.

There are exactly two local boundary section classes, <v> and <cv>.
Their transports are the identity and the specified D58 F-conjugator g.
Brown therefore identifies the boundary map with (a,a_other) ->
(r_S a,r_S a). The possible other global section class contributes
no boundary occurrence. The half-rank calculation forces r_S onto.
A finite self-dual coset module k[J/S] separates any nontrivial commuter,
exactly as in D74, and contradicts surjectivity.

All-unit and recognition maps
For every full unit mu and i=0,1, the transporter is exactly
  a_mu,0 = c^((mu-1)/2) g,
  a_mu,1 = c^((mu+1)/2) g^{-1}.
The original c and both v_i labels are retained. Their homology is
(-1)^i[g], hence remains primitive by D59.

The full remaining equation is c^{-mu}a_mu,i ~_F a_mu,i.
The equivalence with D61's entire conjugator space is spelled out:
the F-conjugator h gives the outside centralizer element hv_i.
In the two-class case its exact even square parameter gives the
unique other commuting reflection w; in the one-class case the
odd square parameter and the full primewise correction give the
unique outside root z. The standard extension basis maps and their
inverse kernel/parity maps are retained in D75.26.

The one-class extension is real projective by the now verified full
self-centralizers and the actual open projective index-two kernel.
The other full fixed group in the two-class case retains D59's
normalizer, H1 dimension one, and full fixed-line surjection.
No procyclic identification of that group or existence of the
remaining peripheral conjugacy is asserted.

No new finite computation is needed. The proof uses the accepted
D74 coefficient mechanism with the exact substitutions displayed,
and elementary identities for every actual full unit.

## Exact endpoint-path and frontier comparison

# Exact reductions after the fixed-frontier extension C63

C63 is complete and has passed root and independent C audits. This separate note records the remaining scope and an auxiliary fact relevant to the zero-dimensional local-centralizer case. It does not modify the registered C63 source or metadata.

## What the compact-family construction establishes at a nonfixed frontier

The global maps in C63's compact family are limits, in the actual global homeomorphism topology and all selected original source-cover topologies, of words in the original loop. They therefore preserve every original open orbit and fix every point fixed by the original generated group. Their projected point and inverse-point control is exact:

\[
K_{\rho,t}(x)\in\mathcal K\mathcal K^{-1}\mathcal K(x),\qquad
K_{\rho,t}^{-1}(x)\in\mathcal K^{-1}\mathcal K\mathcal K^{-1}(x).
\]

At a fixed frontier point, the compact evaluation set on the right is a singleton. At a nonfixed frontier point the proof supplies a compact evaluation set but does not identify it with one prescribed point. The construction also does not establish that arbitrary infinite-product limits preserve each individual nonopen original G-orbit. Consequently the same compactness argument does not, by itself, prove nonfixed-frontier continuity for the selected lift.

The continuous-selection proof was performed in the actual principal real line bundle. Its relative boundary conditions were the four homotopy-parameter faces, where the prescribed coefficients already belong to the original crossing intervals. A prescribed value on a physical nonfixed frontier has not been shown to satisfy an analogous lower-limit inclusion. No relative selection across that frontier is being asserted.

## Proved auxiliary fact: actual small paths move a point through an open neighborhood

This fact does not require a positive-dimensional local centralizer.

Take the countable collection of original open G-orbits. Retain their original universal covers and based lifted loop paths. As in C63, form the compatible product of the global plane homeomorphism group and the homeomorphism groups of those covers, with every map and inverse controlled on compact sets. Let L be the closure, in this exact product, of the subgroup generated by the labelled original loop. It is Polish. Its global values commute with the original operators, preserve every original open orbit, and fix the original G-fixed set. The covering and operator equations remain exact by closedness.

Let P(L) be the group of continuous paths σ:[0,1]→L with σ(0)=1, with pointwise multiplication and the compact-open path topology. It is Polish. A compatible complete metric on L gives completeness of the uniform path metric; uniform limits remain continuous and based. Separability follows by coding finite values from a countable dense subset and a uniform-continuity scale, selecting one actual path from every nonempty coded neighborhood. Pointwise multiplication and inversion are continuous on compact path-parameter sets.

Fix x in an original open orbit O. The action

\[
P(L)\times O\longrightarrow O,\qquad (\sigma,y)\longmapsto\sigma(1)y
\]

is continuous and transitive. Indeed, every original finite word carrying x to a prescribed point of O is the endpoint of an actual based word path: replace each original parameter t_j by s t_j and keep its inverse sign. This path lies in the labelled group L and retains all original source lifts.

The exact Polish orbit-open-map theorem already used in C30 therefore makes

\[
\operatorname{ev}_x:P(L)\longrightarrow O,\qquad
\sigma\longmapsto\sigma(1)x
\]

an open map. For every neighborhood U of the constant identity path in P(L), U·x is an open neighborhood of x in O. Thus every point of that neighborhood can be reached by some actual global operator-centralizing path satisfying all the controls defining U.

For example, U may require the global maps and inverses to move each point of one specified ambient compact set by less than ε at every time, and may simultaneously impose any finite collection of compact controls on the original source-cover maps and their inverses. All these are open conditions in the stated path topology. The resulting motions fix the original G-fixed set throughout and preserve every original open orbit.

This conclusion supplies existence of small actual paths for nearby endpoints. It does not supply a continuous choice of such paths as the endpoint varies, a local section of the evaluation map, or a lift of an arbitrary parameterized deformation. Those additional requirements have not been deduced from openness.

## Current remaining cases

The new fixed-frontier principal-fiber contraction leaves the prescribed-value problem at nonfixed frontier orbits. Open orbits whose full local centralizer is zero-dimensional also lack the principal one-dimensional fiber used by the selection argument. Their intrinsic contractions from C32 remain available, but a controlled global extension through all their frontiers has not been established. C12 remains open.


## Controlled fillings in the original planar domain

# Controlled filling inside the original planar domain

## Proved lemma

Let O be an open subset of the plane, D an open Euclidean disk, and U a connected component of O∩D. For every basepoint in U, the actual inclusion

\[
U\hookrightarrow O
\]

induces an injective homomorphism on fundamental groups. Consequently every loop in U which is null-homotopic in O has a based disk filling entirely inside U, and thus entirely inside the prescribed ambient disk D.

## Proof with the actual intermediate maps

Let γ be a based loop in U which is null-homotopic in O. Its compact image has an open neighborhood in U. A sufficiently fine polygonal approximation γ' and the straight homotopy on each small parameter interval remain in that neighborhood, fixing the basepoint. Choose the finitely many vertices generically if necessary, so that the polygonal image is a finite planar graph.

Take a sufficiently small compact connected planar regular neighborhood N of this graph inside U. It can be constructed as a finite union of small vertex disks and thin edge rectangles, followed by rounding corners. Its boundary consists of one outer Jordan curve and finitely many inner Jordan curves. Its bounded complementary components D_i are the open Jordan disks enclosed by the inner curves.

The closed outer Jordan disk containing N is contained in D. Indeed, the outer boundary is a compact subset of the convex disk D, and its entire bounded interior lies in the convex hull of that boundary: any point outside the convex hull is separated from it by a line and can be joined to infinity without meeting the boundary. The convex hull of a compact subset of the open Euclidean disk stays in that disk. In particular every inner disk D_i lies in D.

Fill into N precisely those D_i which are entirely contained in O, and call the resulting compact planar surface N'. Since each of their boundaries already belongs to N, this means adjoining the complete closed inner disk. Thus

\[
\gamma'\subset N\subset N'\subset O\cap D.
\]

The set N' is connected and meets U, so it is contained in the same component U. For every inner disk still unfilled, choose one point p_i∈D_i\O. These points are distinct and lie in the interiors of the remaining complementary disks.

The actual inclusion

\[
N'\hookrightarrow\mathbb R^2\setminus\{p_i\}
\]

is a homotopy equivalence, as can be seen by an explicit strong deformation retraction. For each remaining closed inner Jordan disk, choose a Schoenflies disk coordinate carrying p_i to the origin and its boundary to the unit circle. In the punctured disk use the radial homotopy

\[
(r,\theta)\longmapsto((1-t)r+t,\theta),\qquad 0<r\le1.
\]

For the exterior of the outer Jordan disk, use its Schoenflies exterior coordinate and the same formula for r≥1. All these homotopies fix their boundary circles pointwise. Together with the identity on N', they paste on the finite closed cover of the punctured plane to a strong deformation retraction onto N'. Every basepoint in N' remains fixed.

The null-homotopy of γ' in O is also a null-homotopy in the punctured plane, because every chosen p_i lies outside O. Applying the displayed deformation retraction gives a based disk filling of γ' in N'. Prepending the based polygonal approximation homotopy gives a filling of γ in U. This proves the asserted injectivity and the controlled filling statement.

## Exact scope for the frontier problem

The filled hull of a loop's image need not itself lie in O: a circle followed by its reverse in an annulus is already a null loop whose image surrounds an excluded point. The lemma above avoids that assertion. It produces a filling in the actual O and in any chosen open Euclidean disk containing the loop within one component of O∩D.

When original evaluation loops shrink uniformly to a fixed frontier point, choose disks with radii tending to zero that contain those loops. Their original ambient-null restrictions are null in O by C32, and each connected loop lies in one component of the intersection. The lemma therefore supplies disk fillings with the same vanishing ambient diameter. It does not provide a continuous selection of the fillings, a simultaneous configuration or edge isotopy, or an equivariant fragmentation of an arbitrary original loop.


## Exact principal-fiber extension of the interval argument

# Proved extension of C62 to the actual principal operator fibers

This note applies the already proved word triangle to another explicitly constructed foliation. It does not assert a lift of a moving base contraction or an ambient extension of the intrinsic C33 contraction.

Let O be an original open orbit of the loop-generated group G, and let C_O be the full local centralizer of G restricted to O with its actual compact-open topology. Suppose its dimension is one, and put A=C_O^0. C30 and C33 prove that C_O is a free proper Lie transformation group and construct a principal bundle

\[
\pi_0:O\longrightarrow B=O/A.
\]

Here A is the additive line or circle, B is a line or circle, and the pair of circles is excluded because O is noncompact. C33 supplies an actual section and the homeomorphism

\[
\Theta:A\times B\longrightarrow O,\qquad
\Theta(a,b)=a\,s(b),\qquad \pi_0\Theta(a,b)=b.
\]

## The original universal cover and its exact labels

Write η_A:R→A and η_B:R→B for the identity in a line factor and the specified quotient by Z in a circle factor. The displayed map

\[
p_*(u,v)=\Theta(\eta_A(u),\eta_B(v))
\]

is a covering from the simply connected plane, hence a universal covering. If p_O:X_O→O is the previously chosen universal covering, choose the point above Θ(0,0) in each cover. The unique based covering isomorphism χ:X_O→R² satisfies

\[
p_O=p_*\chi.
\]

It is a homeomorphism: the inverse is the unique based lift of p_* through p_O, and the two compositions are identity lifts with the same basepoint. Define the actual real quotient

\[
F=\operatorname{pr}_2\chi.
\]

Its connected proper fibers are horizontal lines in this explicit chart. The original deck labels are retained by

\[
\chi\delta\chi^{-1}(u,v)
 =(u+m_A(\delta),v+m_B(\delta)),
\]

where m_A or m_B is identically zero in each line factor, and the circle factor has its integer label determined by the original deck transformation. There is at most one circle factor. In particular

\[
F\delta=F+m_B(\delta).
\]

This is an equation for each original δ, not a replacement of its label by an unchosen covering generator.

## The original loop and all local operator components

Every f_t|O commutes with A. In the actual product chart its exact form is therefore

\[
(a,b)\longmapsto(a+\varphi_t(b),\psi_t(b)),
\]

as in C33.11. Its based source lift has the form

\[
\chi\widetilde f_t^O\chi^{-1}(u,v)
 =(u+\widetilde\varphi_t(v),\beta_t(v)),
\]

where β_t is the actual based real lift of ψ_t and is increasing. The fiber translation lift is fixed by the based source lift, including any nonzero integer accumulated in a circle fiber. No endpoint closure is needed for the C62 triangle argument. Every original deck map commutes with the source lift, by the same deck-valued commutator path used in C62.

Every q∈C_O normalizes its characteristic identity component A and hence preserves the A-orbit partition. Its induced map on B is the actual map of the quotient group C_O/A. C33 proves that this group acts freely and properly on B. A decreasing line or circle homeomorphism has a fixed point, so no decreasing element occurs in this induced group. Its source lifts consequently preserve F-fibers with increasing induced real base maps. The full affine fiber component equations in C33, including a negative conjugation coefficient on A, are retained; no such coefficient is discarded by the base orientation observation.

The original stabilizer H_O maps into C_O by restriction, with the precise kernel of C30.4. No identification of H_O with C_O is made. For an operator h transporting O to O', conjugation by h gives an actual topological group isomorphism C_O→C_O' and carries A-orbits to A'-orbits. Thus the principal partitions are transported along all original operator orbits. The projected global word comparison is automatically natural under h because h commutes with every f_t.

## Exact containment and uniform consequence

The source hypotheses used in the C62 proof are now all verified: the original cover, one actual product homeomorphism, a real quotient with connected proper fibers, the increasing induced base maps of the original lifted path, and the original deck equations. For any x∈O and two original prefix times whose values have exactly the same F-level, the compact interval I in that real fiber satisfies

\[
I\subset
\{\widetilde f_u^O(\widetilde f_v^O)^{-1}
  \widetilde f_a^O\widetilde x:0\le a\le v\le u\le1\}.
\]

Projection gives exactly

\[
p_O(I)\subset
\{f_u f_v^{-1}f_a(x):0\le a\le v\le u\le1\}.
\]

The inverse-path version is the projected word f_u^(-1)f_vf_a^(-1). For arbitrary varying dimension-one principal orbits with x_n→x, the one compact global word map gives the same uniform compact containment and same-G-orbit accumulation as C62, and complete shrinking when x is fixed by G.

This application does not require any actual global quotient to a nonopen orbit. In particular it applies when the full nonopen-target family is empty. It concerns the already established principal orbit fibers, and does not assume that this foliation extends across the frontier. The remaining moving-base lift and its prescribed nonfixed boundary values are unchanged.


## Exact full-prefix fiber-hull selection obstruction

# The full prefix fiber hull need not admit a continuous base lift

This is a limitation of a particular lifting prescription. It supplies neither a failure of C12 nor a failure of lifting the base median itself. C62 remains applicable: the interval hulls considered here lie in the actual three-word images, which are a larger class of allowed values.

## The actual action and original quotient

For positive real λ and real c,d, let

\[
A_{\lambda,c,d}(x,y)=(\lambda x+c,e^d y).
\]

These maps form the closed plane group Aff⁺(R) × R. The coordinates of a homeomorphic limit are recovered by evaluating two points of the horizontal axis and two points of one vertical line; injectivity excludes a zero horizontal or vertical scale. Thus the group is closed with its stated Lie topology. It acts transitively on each open half-plane. Its only nonopen orbit is the horizontal axis B, on which its action is the affine line action.

Take the original operator group H to be the vertical scaling subgroup. Every displayed map commutes with H. On the upper half-plane O use the actual homeomorphism

\[
\chi:O\longrightarrow\mathbb R^2,\qquad
\chi(x,y)=(u,v)=(\log y,x).
\]

The action in this chart is exactly

\[
(u,v)\longmapsto(u+d,\lambda v+c).
\]

At the source point (x,y)=(0,1), the stabilizer consists of the maps with c=d=0. Its fixed set in the sole nonopen orbit B is {(0,0)}. Consequently the full family of actual nontrivial global quotients has exactly one member for that source label:

\[
\Phi(x,y)=(x,0),\qquad F(u,v)=v.
\]

No target or source period occurs.

## A based original loop

Interpolate each of λ,c,d linearly between these parameter values:

| Time | λ | c | d |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| 0 | 1 | 0 | 0 |
| 1/6 | 5/4 | 1 | 2 |
| 1/3 | 3/4 | 1 | 3 |
| 1/2 | 3/2 | 1 | 2 |
| 2/3 | 3/2 | 1 | 3 |
| 5/6 | 1 | 3 | 3 |
| 1 | 1 | 0 | 0 |

The positive values of λ make every interpolated map a homeomorphism. Write f_t for this based loop. It is already null-homotopic in the H-centralizer: replace its parameters by

\[
\lambda_r(t)=1-r+r\lambda(t),\qquad
c_r(t)=r c(t),\qquad d_r(t)=r d(t),
\quad 0\le r\le1.
\]

The subgroup generated by the loop is the whole displayed group. On the segment from 1/2 to 2/3, taking relative maps gives an interval of pure vertical translations in u; these generate the full vertical R. After cancelling this vertical factor, the first segment gives dilations about the line point -4. On the segment from 1/3 to 1/2, the base maps have the form v↦λv+1. Taking a relative map A_λ A_λ₀^(-1) gives v↦(λ/λ₀)v+1-λ/λ₀, which is a dilation about 1; its vertical factor is again cancelled by the already available vertical subgroup. An interval of positive dilation parameters generates every positive parameter, so both complete dilation groups are present. For equal parameters λ, the product of a dilation about p with the inverse dilation about q is the translation by (1-λ)(p-q). The two distinct centers therefore supply an interval of nonzero translations, hence every translation and the full affine group. The original open orbits and quotient just specified are thus exactly those of the loop-generated group.

## The median base and its full-prefix crossings

Fix the prefix length ρ=1/3 and the endpoint time T=5/6. For |v|<1/4 the running maximum of the real base path up to ρ is

\[
M(v)=1+v+\frac{|v|}{4}.
\]

Indeed the affine base coordinate is linear on every time segment, so its prefix maximum is the maximum at the vertices 0,1/6,1/3. The initial value v is below both other values near zero. The chosen endpoint is b_T(v)=v+3>M(v). C27's median base at these parameters is therefore exactly M(v).

For each source point (u,v), now allow **all** original times in the larger prefix [0,T], and take the hull in the real fiber F=M(v) of every crossing

\[
f_s(u,v),\qquad 0\le s\le T,\qquad b_s(v)=M(v).
\]

For 0<v<1/4, the crossing at time 1/6 has fiber shift 2. There is one further crossing on the segment from 1/3 to 1/2. Its fractional position on that segment is exactly

\[
\frac{(1+5v/4)-(1+3v/4)}{(1+3v/2)-(1+3v/4)}
=\frac23.
\]

The fiber shift there is 3+(2-3)(2/3)=7/3. Every other segment is strictly below or strictly above the level except for the already listed shared endpoints. Thus the full-prefix fiber hull has first-coordinate interval

\[
[u+2,u+7/3]\qquad(0<v<1/4).
\]

For -1/4<v<0, the prefix maximum is attained at time 1/3, with fiber shift 3. The segment from 2/3 to T later crosses the same level, but its fiber shift is identically 3. All remaining crossings are the same shared endpoint. Consequently the full-prefix fiber hull is the singleton

\[
\{u+3\}\qquad(-1/4<v<0).
\]

At v=0, all times from 1/6 through 2/3 lie at base level 1; their shifts fill [2,3]. The fiber hull at zero is [u+2,u+3].

## No continuous selection exists from these hulls

Any lift of the specified median base whose value at (u,v) is required to lie in this full-prefix fiber hull has fiber coordinate u+3 for every negative v close to zero, but has fiber coordinate at most u+7/3 for every positive v close to zero. Evaluating at the fixed source value u=0 proves that such a lift cannot be continuous at v=0. The larger hull at v=0 cannot change these incompatible one-sided limits.

This obstruction permits an arbitrary choice anywhere in the whole filled interval and allows the entire original prefix up to T, not just the shorter prefix that defines the running maximum. Thus pointwise interval filling alone does not supply a transverse continuous lifting rule. The explicit null-homotopy above shows exactly why this is a limitation of that rule rather than a failure of the underlying centralizer problem.

The affine equations, maximum comparisons, complete crossing list, and exact fiber shifts are checked symbolically in `code/prefix_fiber_hull_selection_certificate.py` and its adjacent JSON output. The continuity contradiction is the one-sided limit proof above.


## Exact scope and topology of the infinite-action comparison

# Audit of the infinite-operator obligation in Part C(b)

This audit concerns the exact comparison used in Part C, with the compact-open topology. It records an unresolved obligation in the current return; it makes no claim that the unresolved statement is open in the literature.

## 1. The specified source and target

The source in scan 264, folio 133, consists of finite disjoint unions of surfaces `U = X \\ S`, where `X` is compact, `S` is finite, and every genus-zero component of `X` meets `S`. The exclusion of an unpunctured sphere is explicit in both the definition and the subsequent notation `(g,ν) ≠ (0,0)`. I checked the supplied scan `background/scans/140-2/scan264.jpg` against `background/longue_marche/LM_P027/LM_P027_checked_fixed.tex`.

The same definition is used in C.1. In particular, the connected abelian objects admitted there are the plane, the annulus, and the unpunctured torus. Part A's separate audit of all four exceptional numerical types also includes `(0,0)`; that does not change the source object condition of the Part C functor.

The first sentence of C(b) requires the four-level theorem for finite operator groups on every hyperbolic type. Its last sentence asks for infinite-group failures at each level. Read within C(a)'s fixed functor, the latter request requires an **admissible** surface. The sentence does not expressly require the infinite example at each level to be hyperbolic, so an example on an admitted abelian type would satisfy that existential request. C.12's hyperbolic question is therefore a more specific remaining instance. No example in any admitted type has yet been supplied for level 0.

## 2. The exact map at level 0

Let `A = Homeo(X,S)` and let `u:Γ→A` be a strict action of an arbitrary discrete group. Put `H=u(Γ)`, `M=C_A(H)`, and base `M` at the identity. Let `N` be the weak automorphism groupoid of this action in the categorical group `Π₁A`, with its identity weak automorphism as base object. An automorphism of that base object is a loop class `z∈π₁(A,1)` satisfying the naturality equality

`u(γ) z u(γ)⁻¹ = z` for every `γ∈Γ`.

Consequently

`π₁N = π₁(A,1)^Γ`.

The comparison sends an actual centralizing loop to the same loop in `A`. Its exact map is

`ι_*: π₁(C_A(H),1) → π₁(A,1)^Γ`,

where `ι` is subgroup inclusion. The image lies in the displayed invariants because the representatives commute pointwise with every `u(γ)`. Finiteness of `Γ` is not used in this groupoid calculation. For a transporter based at any equivariant homeomorphism, translation by that homeomorphism identifies its based fundamental-group map with the corresponding automorphism-space map.

Every based loop in `C_A(H)` lies in `A⁰`, and every based homotopy does too. Thus its source is also `π₁(C_{A⁰}(H),1)`. On a hyperbolic type, the already proved contractibility of `A⁰` makes the target zero, and level 0 is exactly the vanishing of this source. For the plane, annulus, and torus, the targets are respectively the invariant subgroups of `Z`, `Z`, and `Z²`. Nonzero fundamental group of the actual centralizer alone does not establish failure in those three cases: the kernel of the displayed inclusion map is the required datum.

| C.11, second construction | The same surfaces and `Γ=Z` | The nontrivial radial-rotation action and the trivial action have the same homomorphism to `π₀A` but cannot be conjugate | The independent lift-uniqueness obstruction at level 2 supplied |

| C.25 | Unpunctured sphere; faithful irrational-rotation action of `Γ=Z` | `Z→Z/2`, `n↦n mod 2`, with kernel `2Z` | Valid for the separately enlarged `Π₁Homeo(S²)` comparison; the sphere is not a C.1 object |

C.3 defines cumulative levels. The table additionally identifies the separate connectedness, uniqueness, and existence obstructions requested by the prompt; it does not infer a nonzero fundamental group from disconnectedness.

For finite operator groups, C.10 treats the admitted exceptional types with their coherent peripheral data and proves all four corrected levels. The sphere calculations C.24 and C.25 concern their expressly specified extended comparisons.

## 4. Exact relationship of the sphere and punctured comparisons

Let `R_α` be an irrational rotation of `S²` with poles `p_+,p_-`. Its identity centralizer is the same subgroup `C` inside each of

`A₂ = Homeo(S²,{p_+,p_-})`, `A₁ = Homeo(S²,{p_+})`, and `A₀ = Homeo(S²)`.

Indeed, a commuting homeomorphism preserves the set of fixed points `{p_+,p_-}`, and a path from the identity cannot permute this finite set. The angular/latitude parametrization of C.25 gives `C ≃ S¹`, with a full axis rotation as generator. Inclusions induced by forgetting marks yield the commutative diagram on based fundamental groups

```text
π₁C = Z  ──identity──>  π₁A₂ = Z  ──identity──>  π₁A₁ = Z  ──mod 2──>  π₁A₀ = Z/2.
```

Each identity assertion follows from the same rotation circle: it generates both marked homeomorphism groups by the plane and annulus comparisons proved in C.10. The last map is the inclusion of the axis-rotation circle into `SO(3)` under the established homeomorphism-group homotopy equivalence. The irrational rotation is joined to the identity through the same axis rotations in all three groups, so conjugation acts trivially on all these fundamental groups. The displayed groups are therefore the actual weak-transporter target groups as well.

There are no further finite invariant puncture sets to try for this action. A nonpolar point has an infinite orbit under `R_α`, whereas both poles are fixed. Thus deleting one or two poles gives admitted examples with zero kernel; deleting a finite invariant set cannot convert this particular action into a hyperbolic example.

There is a second target comparison that must also be stated explicitly. Keeping C.1's peripheral-diagram recipe while adding `S²` gives the diagram `∅→*`. Its equivalence 2-group is terminal. The actual induced categorical-group map is

`Π₁Homeo(S²) → Eq(∅→*) = 1`.

On identity 2-automorphisms it sends `Z/2→0`; it already fails faithfulness for the trivial operator group. For the irrational action the sphere peripheral-diagram map factors as

`Z → Z/2 → 0`.

C.25 computes the first arrow. This proves the exact relationship between its target and the target obtained by extending the peripheral recipe, without identifying them.

## 5. The compact-open topology and the universal-lift reduction

For this finite-type setup, restriction

`r: Homeo(X,S) → Homeo(X\\S)`

is a topological-group isomorphism for the compact-open topologies. Every homeomorphism of `U=X\\S` extends uniquely to the finite end compactification `X`, so `r` is an algebraic bijection. Restriction is continuous. For completeness, continuity of its inverse can be checked directly. Choose disjoint connected target end neighborhoods `W_j` of diameter below a prescribed uniform tolerance in `X`, and let the complementary target core `C` be compact in `U`. Choose a compact source core `K` large enough that `f₀⁻¹(C)` lies in its interior, and connected source tails `V_i` outside `K` with `f₀(V_i)⊂W_{σ(i)}`. The compact-open condition `f⁻¹(C)⊂int K` is open because inversion is continuous in the homeomorphism group of the locally compact, locally connected surface `U`. Choose one point `z_i` in each `V_i` and impose the open conditions `f(z_i)∈W_{σ(i)}`. Connectedness now gives `f(V_i)⊂W_{σ(i)}`: the image avoids `C` and contains that prescribed point. This controls the extensions at all ends uniformly. Ordinary compact-open control on the complementary compact core controls the rest of `X`. Translating this neighborhood argument from `f₀` to any homeomorphism proves continuity of `r⁻¹`.

The canonical-lift map in the paragraph after C.12 is consequently a continuous injective homomorphism for the same topology and same ambient group used throughout Part C:

`j:A⁰→Homeo(Ũ)`, `p j(f)=f p`, `j(f)δ=δj(f)` for every deck transformation `δ∈π₁U`.

Centre-freeness gives uniqueness of the commuting lift, and proves the exact equality

`j(C_{A⁰}(H)) = j(A⁰) ∩ C_{Homeo(Ũ)}(H̃)`,

where `H̃` is the group of all lifts of elements of `H`. In particular, based centralizer loops lift to closed loops. This equality provides no null-homotopy in that intersection. For arbitrary `H`, metric geodesic interpolation need not commute with `H̃` and need not consist of homeomorphisms. Those are separate required properties, and neither has been established by the lift comparison.

## 6. Exact remaining obligation

The current examples supply admitted infinite-group failures for levels 1, 2, and 3. They do not yet supply an admitted infinite-group action for which

`ker[π₁(C_A(u(Γ)),1) → π₁(A,1)^Γ] ≠ 0`.

The hyperbolic instance is the question whether an arbitrary subgroup `H≤Homeo(X,S)` can have `π₁(C_{A⁰}(H),1)≠0`. No general injectivity theorem and no such counterexample is proved in the present return. C.25 should remain as its valid sphere computation, with this scope statement attached, rather than being reported as completion of C(b)'s admissible zero-level request.


## Centralizer-specific reductions and a closed-subgroup obstruction

# Auxiliary results for the remaining centralizer question

These results do not settle C.12. They identify an obstruction to a closed-subgroup-only argument and prove reductions for transitive actions, intrinsic one-dimensional orbits, proper open-orbit centralizers, and globally commuting or locally compact loop-generated groups. The numbered chain now runs through C.50. C.42–C.50 have passed root and independent C review; C.46 and C.48 are the parent's fragments in work/root, and the others are in the adjacent TeX files. All homeomorphism groups have the compact-open topology. The target remains the kernel of the inclusion-induced map in c_scope_audit.md.

## 1. Closed torsion-free subgroups can have nonzero fundamental group

There is a closed torsion-free Polish subgroup of `Homeo(D²,∂D²)` whose fundamental group is `Z`. Consequently the assertions that the hyperbolic identity homeomorphism group is contractible, that a centralizer is closed, and that there are no nontrivial compact circle actions do not, by themselves, prove C.12.

Here is a construction, including its topology. Let

`E = {f∈L¹([0,1],R) : f is integer-valued almost everywhere}`,

with addition and the `L¹` metric. This is a closed subgroup of the separable Banach space `L¹`: every `L¹`-convergent sequence has an almost-everywhere convergent subsequence, and an almost-everywhere limit of integer-valued functions is integer-valued. Hence `E` is a Polish group. For `0<s≤1` put

`D_s f(t)=f(t/s)` on `[0,s]`, and `D_s f(t)=0` on `(s,1]`; put `D_0 f=0`.

Then `||D_s f||₁=s||f||₁`. Dilation is strongly continuous on `L¹`, first for step functions and then by norm approximation, so `(s,f)↦D_s f` is continuous. It contracts `E` to zero and preserves each open norm ball about zero. Thus `E` is contractible and locally contractible.

The subgroup of constant integer functions is a closed discrete copy of `Z`. The quotient

`G=E/Z`

is a Polish topological group, and `E→G` is a covering map: translates by distinct integers of a ball of radius less than `1/3` are disjoint. Since `E` is simply connected, this is the universal cover and `π₁G=Z`. An explicit generator is

`s↦[1_[0,s]]`, `0≤s≤1`.

Its lift starts at the zero function and ends at the constant function `1`. On the other hand, `G` is torsion-free: `n[f]=0` means `nf` is a constant integer, so the integer-valued function `f` is constant and `[f]=0`.

Let `V=L¹([0,1],R)/R`, where the denominator consists of all constant real functions. The natural homomorphism

`G→V`, `[f]_Z↦[f]_R`,

is an isometric embedding for the quotient metrics. Indeed, for an integer-valued `h`, the function `c↦∫|h-c|` is linear on each interval between consecutive integers, and achieves its minimum at an integer median. Therefore

`inf_(c∈R)||h-c||₁ = inf_(n∈Z)||h-n||₁`.

The quotient metric on `G` is complete: for a Cauchy subsequence with successive quotient distances below `2^-k`, choose successive representatives differing by integer constants so that their `L¹` distances are below `2^-k`; these representatives converge in `E`. The whole Cauchy sequence then converges in `G`. The image in the Banach space `V` is consequently closed.

For clarity, the usual linear embedding of a separable Banach space into `C([0,1],R)` can be obtained without any change of topology. Let `K` be the weak-star compact metrizable unit ball of `V*`. The map `v↦(ℓ↦ℓ(v))` is an isometric linear embedding `V→C(K)`. A continuous surjection from the Cantor set to `K`, followed by linear interpolation on the complementary intervals of the Cantor set, gives an isometric linear embedding into `C([0,1])`. Place that interval in the middle third of `[0,1]` and interpolate linearly to zero at the two outside endpoints. This gives a closed isometric linear embedding

`T:V→C₀([0,1],R)`,

where `C₀` here means functions vanishing at both endpoints. Existence of the Cantor surjection follows by nested finite covers of the compact metric space with diameters tending to zero; pullback preserves the sup norm because the map is onto. The interpolation also preserves the sup norm.

On the rectangle `[-1,1]×[0,1]`, define, for `F∈C₀([0,1])`,

`Φ_F(x,y)=(tanh(artanh(x)+F(y)), y)` for `|x|<1`,

and fix the vertical sides. It fixes the whole boundary and satisfies `Φ_F Φ_Q=Φ_(F+Q)`. The map `F↦Φ_F` is continuous because `tanh` is Lipschitz. Its inverse on its image is continuous: evaluation at `(0,y)` recovers `tanh(F(y))`; near any given bounded profile, applying `artanh` recovers `F` uniformly. Its image is closed in the homeomorphism group. In fact a homeomorphism limit preserves the horizontal leaves and their endpoints; the image of the compact segment `{0}×[0,1]` stays a positive distance from the two vertical sides, so the recovered profiles are uniformly bounded and converge uniformly. This proves closedness as well as inverse continuity.

The composite `G→V→C₀([0,1])→Homeo(D²,∂D²)` is therefore a closed topological-group embedding. Extending its elements by the identity outside a closed coordinate disc gives the same kind of embedding into `Homeo₀(U)` for every hyperbolic surface `U`. The extension is a topological embedding because restriction to the compact disc recovers the original maps, and its image is closed because being the identity off that disc is a closed condition. Its generator is ambient-null but nontrivial in `G`.

**This construction cannot itself be the proposed full centralizer.** Its loop is already null in the larger real shear group by the displayed profile contraction. C.39 gives the stronger exact comparison: because the loop has commuting values and is ambient-null, it contracts in every centralizer containing those values. Hence the subgroup, whose generator is nonzero internally, cannot equal such a full centralizer. It remains a proved obstruction to reasoning from closedness, torsion-freeness, or the absence of circle subgroups alone.

## 2. A transitive centralizer cannot give a closed hyperbolic counterexample

For a closed orientable surface `X` of genus at least two, the centralizer in `Homeo(X)` of any transitive subgroup is finite.

Let `K` be the closure of that subgroup in `Homeo(X)`. It remains transitive and is Polish, and taking the closure does not change its centralizer `N`. Fix `x∈X` and put `K_x={k∈K:kx=x}`. The continuous transitive action of the Polish group `K` on the compact metrizable space `X` has an open evaluation map `K→X`. This is the ordinary-action case of the open mapping principle; a primary proof is Gómez–Pinedo–Uzcátegui, *The open mapping principle for partial actions of Polish groups*, arXiv:1709.01977, Theorem 3.5 (the domain for an ordinary action is all of `K`). Thus `K/K_x→X` is a homeomorphism.

Every element of `N` is determined by its value at `x`: commutation gives `n(kx)=k n(x)`. Its possible values are precisely

`N_K(K_x)/K_x ⊂ K/K_x = X`.

For the forward inclusion, `n(x)=kx` implies `K_x=K_(kx)=kK_xk⁻¹`. Conversely, an element of the normalizer gives the well-defined equivariant homeomorphism `gK_x↦gkK_x`. The normalizer of a closed subgroup is closed, so this subset of `X` is closed and hence compact.

Evaluation `N→X` is a homeomorphism onto this closed subset, not merely a continuous injection. Here is the uniform-continuity check. For any `y=g_y x` and a chosen `ε>0`, take a neighborhood `W_y` of the identity in `K` such that every `g_y w g_y⁻¹`, `w∈W_y`, moves all points of `X` by less than `ε/3`. The sets `g_y W_y x` are open neighborhoods of `y`, so finitely many cover `X`, with corresponding elements `g_1,…,g_m`. If `n(x)` is sufficiently close to `n₀(x)`, then each `g_i n(x)` is within `ε/3` of `g_i n₀(x)`. For `z=g_i w x` in the corresponding neighborhood, commutation gives

`n(z)=g_i w n(x)` and `n₀(z)=g_i w n₀(x)`.

The two small movements by `g_i w g_i⁻¹` and the middle `ε/3` bound show `d(n(z),n₀(z))<ε` uniformly in `z`. This proves inverse continuity. Hence `N` is compact.

The action of `N` is free in the sense that only the identity can fix a point: transitivity and commutation propagate one fixed point to all of `X`. Every homeomorphism isotopic to the identity on this closed surface has Lefschetz number `χ(X)=2-2g≠0`, and therefore has a fixed point. Thus

`N∩Homeo₀(X)=1`.

Projection to the discrete mapping-class group is consequently an injective continuous homomorphism on the compact group `N`. Its image is compact and discrete, hence finite. This proves the assertion.

In particular, when the subgroup generated by the values of a based centralizer loop is transitive on `X`, the operator image is contained in this finite centralizer. The finite-action theorem already proved in Part C then contracts the loop equivariantly. Therefore a closed-hyperbolic counterexample cannot arise from a loop whose generated subgroup is transitive. This argument does not classify the lower-dimensional or nonclosed orbits of a general path-generated subgroup, and it supplies no general contraction in that case.

Primary source checked for the open mapping step: https://arxiv.org/pdf/1709.01977, especially the introduction and Theorem 3.5. The rest of this section is the explicit evaluation/normalizer argument above.

## 3. Line centralizers: the exact contraction in C.27

For an arbitrary subgroup \(H\leq\operatorname{Homeo}(\mathbb R)\), every based loop \(f_t\) in its orientation-preserving centralizer has the contraction

\[
m_r(x)=\min_{0\leq u\leq r}f_u(x),\qquad
M_r(x)=\max_{0\leq u\leq r}f_u(x),\qquad
Q_{s,t}(x)=\operatorname{med}(m_{1-s}(x),f_t(x),M_{1-s}(x)).
\]

All maps in this formula are increasing homeomorphisms. For strict increase, the compact family supplies the positive gap \(\min_u(f_u(y)-f_u(x))>0\) whenever \(x<y\); compactness of the inverse family supplies uniform properness at both ends. Compact-uniform continuity gives continuity of the extrema in all parameters. An increasing commuting homeomorphism preserves the extrema; a decreasing one interchanges them. Every line homeomorphism preserves the median, so the contraction commutes with all of \(H\), including its orientation-reversing elements. The initial parameter is the original loop, the final parameter is the identity, and the two endpoints of each loop remain the identity. This proves \(\pi_1 C_{\operatorname{Homeo}^+(\mathbb R)}(H)=0\) without a finiteness or regularity assumption on \(H\). The formula is natural under every line conjugacy.

## 4. The exact plane-orbit reduction in C.28

For a based plane homeomorphism loop, put \(P=\{f_t\}\cup\{f_t^{-1}\}\) and \(G=\langle P\rangle\). Each orbit is exhausted by the nested compact Peano continua \(K_n=P^n x\). Every orbit is open, a point, a Jordan curve, or an intrinsically ordered line that has a continuous injective parametrization by \(\mathbb R\) embedding every compact interval.

The proof does not assume an orbit foliation. If an orbit contains a Jordan curve that some group element moves, an isotopy to that element sweeps the curve across an open set: points in the symmetric difference of the endpoint Jordan domains have different winding numbers, so the curve must pass through them. Transitivity makes the whole orbit open. If the group preserves that curve, it is the entire orbit.

In the remaining case each \(K_n\) is a dendrite. A countable dense set in a dendrite determines each branch point by three points in different complementary components, so its branch points are countable. Every triod point of the whole orbit is already a branch point of some \(K_n\), because its three outer endpoints determine the unique connecting arcs. Transitivity excludes this countable invariant set on a nontrivial arcwise connected orbit. Thus the \(K_n\) are nested arcs, and transitivity also excludes their at most two global endpoints. A cofinal exhaustion by arcs lying in each other's interiors gives the intrinsic line parametrization.

The restricted loop is continuous in this intrinsic line topology: a compact orbit arc lies in some \(K_m\), and all its images lie in the compact arc \(K_{m+1}\), where intrinsic and ambient topologies agree. A commuting operator sends \(K_n(x)\) exactly to \(K_n(hx)\), so it induces a homeomorphism of intrinsic lines. This is the precise map allowing the C.27 contraction on each such orbit. It does not establish that the global subspace topology of a nonclosed line orbit agrees with its intrinsic topology, or that the collection of orbits is a foliation.

## 5. Circle centralizers and the winding map in C.29

For arbitrary \(H\leq\operatorname{Homeo}(S^1)\), the inclusion
\(\pi_1 C_{\operatorname{Homeo}^+(S^1)}(H)\to\pi_1\operatorname{Homeo}^+(S^1)=\mathbb Z\)
is injective. Its image lies in the invariants of \(\mathbb Z\) under the orientation character of \(H\).

The exact covering group is
\(\{F\in\operatorname{Homeo}^+(\mathbb R):F(x+1)=F(x)+1\}\).
It is contractible by real linear interpolation with the identity, and its quotient by integer translations is \(\operatorname{Homeo}^+(S^1)\). A winding-zero loop has a unique based lift which is again a loop. Its commutator with any lift of any element of \(H\) is an integer translation depending continuously on time, hence is identically trivial. C.27 contracts this lifted loop while commuting with every operator lift and with the integer deck translation. It therefore descends to the asserted circle contraction. Median naturality makes this construction independent of circle coordinates, including reversed coordinates.

For a plane loop preserving a Jordan curve, a Schoenflies coordinate sends the curve to the unit circle. Evaluate at two distinct points and take the direction of their difference:

\[
\operatorname{Homeo}^+(\mathbb R^2)\longrightarrow
\operatorname{Conf}_2(\mathbb R^2)\longrightarrow S^1.
\]

If the angular lifts of their images satisfy
\(\alpha(t)<\beta(t)<\alpha(t)+2\pi\), both acquire the same increment \(2\pi n\), and

\[
e^{i\beta}-e^{i\alpha}
=2i e^{i(\alpha+\beta)/2}\sin((\beta-\alpha)/2).
\]

Thus this evaluated loop has exactly the restriction winding \(n\). An ambient-null plane loop maps to a null loop under these continuous maps, so \(n=0\). The canonical lift of a hyperbolic centralizer loop is ambient-null because it comes from the contractible hyperbolic identity homeomorphism group. Consequently C.29 contracts its restriction to every circle orbit, naturally with respect to all commuting operators between such orbits.

## 6. Proper open-orbit centralizers in C.30

For a closed Polish group \(K\) acting transitively on a connected second-countable surface \(V\), let \(N=C_{\operatorname{Homeo}(V)}(K)\). Evaluation gives a homeomorphism from \(N\) onto the closed subset \(N_K(K_x)/K_x\) of \(V=K/K_x\). Multiplication is reversed under this identification: the equivariant map attached to \(aK_x\) is right translation \(kK_x\mapsto kaK_x\).

Here is the extra argument needed for noncompact surfaces. Open evaluation, from the same verified Theorem 3.5 used in C.26, implies sequential microtransitivity: whenever \(x_j\to x\), choose \(k_j\to1\) with \(k_jx=x_j\) by a decreasing identity-neighborhood basis. This uses no local continuous section. If \(n_jx\to nx\), and \(y_j\to y=ax\) stays in a compact subset of \(V\), choose \(b_j\to1\) with \(b_jy=y_j\). Commutation gives

\[
n_jy_j=b_ja n_jx\longrightarrow a n x=n y.
\]

This proves compact-open inverse continuity of evaluation; a direct identical argument also controls inverses. Thus \(N\) is locally compact. It acts freely because one fixed point propagates by transitivity. If \(x_j\to x\) and \(n_jx_j\to y\), then

\[
n_jx=k_j^{-1}n_jx_j\longrightarrow y.
\]

Closedness of the evaluation image gives an element \(n\) with \(nx=y\), and inverse continuity gives \(n_j\to n\). Subsequence extraction over compact subsets of \(V\times V\) proves properness of \((n,z)\mapsto(z,nz)\).

For an open orbit \(O\) from C.28, use the closure of the restricted loop-generated group in \(\operatorname{Homeo}(O)\) as \(K\). The setwise operator stabilizer has the explicit restriction homomorphism \(h\mapsto h|_O\), whose kernel consists of operators fixing \(O\) pointwise. Its image is contained in \(N\), and the closure of that image has the asserted free proper action. This statement retains the compact-open image topology. It does not claim that a dense image regarded as a discrete group acts properly, or that the original group has trivial restriction kernel.

## 7. Fixed-stratum continuity in C.31

The orbitwise C.27/C.29 construction has an exact inverse identity:
\(Q[f]^{-1}=Q[f^{-1}]\). Inversion interchanges the pointwise minimum and maximum of a compact family of increasing line homeomorphisms, and the median is self-dual. The same identity descends through the circle covering. Consequently, on the union \(Y\) of nonopen orbits, the constructed maps and their inverses satisfy

\[
Q_{s,t}(x)\in\{f_u(x):0\leq u\leq1\},\qquad
Q_{s,t}^{-1}(x)\in\{f_u^{-1}(x):0\leq u\leq1\}.
\]

The common fixed set \(Z\) is fixed throughout. Compactness of the two original families gives uniform continuity at every point of \(Z\), for all contraction parameters. This proves continuity of both families along approaches within \(Y\) to \(Z\). It does not prove continuity at a nonfixed line or circle orbit, or control a contraction arriving from an open orbit.

## 8. Discrete proper operators and restriction in C.32

For a free proper action of a **discrete** group \(Q\) on a connected noncompact second-countable boundaryless surface \(O\), the quotient \(p:O\to V=O/Q\) is an actual regular covering with deck group \(Q\). Properness gives only finitely many translates of a compact neighborhood meeting it; freeness then allows a smaller neighborhood disjoint from all of its nonidentity translates. These are the evenly covered neighborhoods.

The exact homeomorphism-group input is Yagasaki, *Homotopy types of homeomorphism groups of noncompact 2-manifolds*, [arXiv:math/0010223](https://arxiv.org/pdf/math/0010223), Theorem 1.1 and compact-pair Lemma 2.1. For a one-point subpolyhedron, the identity component of the point-fixing homeomorphism group is contractible except for the sphere, projective plane and plane among boundaryless surfaces. Local point-moving sections give the evaluation fibration, so evaluation on the homeomorphism group induces an injection on \(\pi_1\) for every other surface. A noncompact connected covering cannot have sphere or projective-plane base; a plane base forces the trivial covering.

A centralizer loop \(f_t\) descends by the exact equation \(b_tp=pf_t\). If \(f_t\) is null in \(\operatorname{Homeo}(O)\), its evaluation loop is null, hence the evaluation loop of \(b_t\) is null and the evaluation injection fills \(b_t\) in \(\operatorname{Homeo}(V)\). For a filling \(B:D^2\to\operatorname{Homeo}(V)\) based at the identity, lift the map

\[
(x,z)\longmapsto B_z(p(x))
\]

on \(O\times D^2\), starting with the identity lift. The covering criterion applies because its fundamental-group image is exactly \(p_*\pi_1(O)\). Lifting the inverse family and using uniqueness proves that every lift is a homeomorphism. The same uniqueness proves exact agreement with \(f_t\) on the boundary and commutation with every deck operator. This establishes the inclusion injection \(\pi_1 C_{\operatorname{Homeo}(O)}(Q)\to\pi_1\operatorname{Homeo}(O)\).

C.32 also proves that an ambient-null plane loop preserving any connected open \(O\) restricts to a null loop there. Yagasaki contracts the identity homeomorphism group unless \(O\) is a plane or open annulus. In those exceptions the group has fundamental group \(\mathbb Z\), and the C.29 two-point winding map detects a model rotation with value one. That map extends to all ambient plane homeomorphisms, so the ambient-null loop has zero restricted class. This includes the plane exception, whose point-evaluation map by itself would not detect its rotation loop.

## 9. All proper open-orbit operators in C.33

For every closed locally compact \(Q\leq\operatorname{Homeo}(O)\) acting freely and properly on a connected noncompact second-countable boundaryless surface, the same inclusion is \(\pi_1\)-injective. C.32 handles the discrete case.

The remaining structural inputs have now been checked directly in primary sources. Pardon, *The Hilbert–Smith conjecture for three-manifolds*, [author PDF](https://web.math.princeton.edu/~jpardon/manuscripts/08_hilbertsmith.pdf), Theorem 1.5 with the introductory reduction, makes \(Q\) a Lie group by applying the theorem to the explicit faithful action \(q(x,s)=(qx,s)\) on \(O\times\mathbb R\). Palais, *On the existence of slices for actions of non-compact Lie groups*, DOI [10.2307/1970335](https://doi.org/10.2307/1970335), Theorem 2.3.3, supplies the free principal-bundle charts. The original author-uploaded text was checked. The same charts and their inverse are explicitly proved in Kramer–Murat García, *Fibrations and Coset Spaces for Locally Compact Groups*, DOI [10.1007/s00031-025-09903-5](https://doi.org/10.1007/s00031-025-09903-5), Theorem 3.7. No smooth structure on the action is assumed.

If \(\dim Q^0=2\), its orbits are open by invariance of domain, hence it is transitive. Freeness forces \(Q=Q^0\), and the centralizer is the group of right translations. Evaluation is a homeomorphism to \(O\), proving the needed injection.

If \(\dim Q^0=1\), put \(A=Q^0=\mathbb R\) or \(\mathbb R/\mathbb Z\). Its quotient is genuinely a one-manifold. The elementary verification is that a slice \(S\) with \(S\times(-1,1)\) a surface is locally path connected, and each open embedded arc in \(S\) is open by invariance of domain. At a point, an arc either has a second branch, giving an open arc through that point, or gives a half-interval neighborhood, whose product has the wrong local second homology. Thus the quotient is a line or circle. The principal \(A\)-bundle is trivial: local interval sections can be joined; on a circle, connectedness of \(A\) corrects the endpoint discrepancy. This gives the exact coordinates \(O=A\times B\).

The component group \(\Delta=Q/A\) acts freely and properly on \(B\). Freeness follows by correcting a fibre displacement with an element of \(A\); properness follows by lifting convergent basepoints through local sections, correcting representatives by \(A\), and applying properness on \(O\). Hence \(\Delta\) is trivial, is \(\mathbb Z\) acting by translation on a line, or is a finite cyclic group acting by rotation on a circle. In the latter case \(A=\mathbb R\), because the circle-times-circle total space would be compact.

A representative generator has the exact form \(q(a,b)=(\lambda a+\tau(b),\beta b)\). On a line base, choosing \(c(b+1)=\lambda c(b)+\tau(b)\) and replacing \(a\) by \(a-c(b)\) removes \(\tau\). On a circle base, write \(q^n=T_{a_0}\). The relations imply \(\lambda^n=1\) and \(\lambda a_0=a_0\). For \(\lambda=1\), replacing \(q\) by \(T_{-a_0/n}q\) makes \(q^n=1\). For \(\lambda=-1\), \(n\) is even and \(a_0=0\) already. Averaging the resulting finite affine action gives a continuous invariant fibre origin. Thus both signs of \(\lambda\) are retained in the coordinate formula \(q(a,b)=(\lambda a,\beta b)\).

The centralizer is then exactly

\[
h(a,b)=(a+\phi(b),\psi(b)),\qquad
\phi(\beta b)=\lambda\phi(b),\qquad
\psi\beta=\beta\psi.
\]

These coordinates give a topological product of the indicated function space and base centralizer; the inverse is \((a,b)\mapsto(a-\phi(\psi^{-1}b),\psi^{-1}b)\). Real-valued \(\phi\) contracts by scaling. For circle-valued \(\phi\), the evaluation of an ambient-null loop makes its based real lift a loop, and the integer discrepancy in its lifted equivariance equation vanishes by continuity from time zero; scaling that lift gives the contraction. C.27 contracts the line-base loop; C.29 contracts the circle-base loop because evaluation forces winding zero. The circle fibre with an additional \(\mathbb Z\)-translation on its line base is included with its exact translation-centralizer constraint.

Combining C.30, C.32 and C.33 gives an intrinsic equivariant contraction on **every open orbit** of the canonical hyperbolic plane loop, retaining the kernel of the original restriction homomorphism. These contractions have no established uniform frontier estimate.

## 10. Full nonopen-locus continuity in C.34

C.34 proves the previously missing continuity at all nonfixed line and circle strata. The proof uses the path action to exclude folds; convergence of compact sets alone is never used to infer their order.

At any nonfixed point \(q\), take a component \((a,b)\) of the times for which \(f_t(q)\ne q\). A short path

\[
g_s=f_{a+s\eta}f_a^{-1}
\]

starts at the identity and moves \(q\) a nonzero amount in one intrinsic direction. Its inverse path supplies the other direction. Shortening the interval keeps both paths arbitrarily small on compact sets and on the intrinsic neighborhood of \(q\). Each value is a product of two original generators, so it takes \(K_n(x)\) into \(K_{n+2}(x)\); the C.28 argument proves intrinsic continuity of all these point tracks.

Straighten a compact arc of a limit orbit. If nearby intrinsic arcs fold a definite distance past the interval between their limiting endpoints, choose a horizontal minimum or maximum on the offending subarc. At a minimum, both endpoints stay a definite distance to the right. In a small rectangle around the limiting minimum, the two first-exit points in the intrinsic directions therefore lie on the right edge. The intervening cap is an intrinsic subarc whose endpoints are outside the open rectangle. A short group path at the nonfixed limiting point moves it left while remaining in that rectangle. By compact-family continuity the same holds for nearby minimum points. Their intrinsically continuous paths cannot leave the cap without meeting one of its two first-exit endpoints, so their endpoints cannot move left of its minimum. This contradiction excludes the fold. The same argument excludes a whole Jordan curve collapsing to a nondegenerate interval.
